### LWB-R6-007 — keyword escaping and parameter construction (2026-09-08)

**RECOVERED source identity and tools.** The verified reference remains `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. `tools/inspect_lwbridge_map_keyword.py` uses `pefile 2024.8.26` and `Capstone 5.0.6`, installed from their pinned PyPI packages in the recorded user-site locations. Reproduce setup with `python -m pip install --user capstone==5.0.6 pefile==2024.8.26`, then run `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_keyword.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe --json`. Durable output is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-08-r6-map-keyword.json`.

**Exact locators and result.** The map-search function spans preferred-image VA `0x140271864`–`0x140276D7B`. At raw PE file offset `0x00C89A56` / xref VA `0x140272AD8`, the original uses the 176-byte predicate `(name LIKE ? ESCAPE '\' COLLATE NOCASE OR alliance_name LIKE ? ESCAPE '\' COLLATE NOCASE OR uuid LIKE ? ESCAPE '\' COLLATE NOCASE OR data_json LIKE ? ESCAPE '\' COLLATE NOCASE)`. The caller then invokes the same replacement routine three times in this exact order: backslash to `\\` (`0x00C89B06`, xref `0x140272B30`, character immediate `0x140272B4A`), percent to `\%` (`0x00C89B08`, xref `0x140272B5D`, immediate `0x140272B74`), and underscore to `\_` (`0x00C89B0A`, xref `0x140272B87`, immediate `0x140272B9E`). The bounded replacement helper at `0x1402948C1` copies each two-byte replacement.

The format descriptor at file offset `0x00C83ECF`, referenced at VA `0x140272BDD`, places one literal percent on each side of the fully escaped value. The loop at `0x140272BF9`–`0x140272C60` initializes a count of four and clones the resulting string once for each SQL placeholder. Therefore a nonempty keyword is a case-insensitive **literal substring**, not a caller-controlled wildcard, across `name`, `alliance_name`, `uuid` and raw `data_json`.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapDataQueryContract` now admits nonempty keyword strings for all recovered map kinds. `MapDataStore.SearchIndexed` performs the recovered replacements and percent wrapping, binds four identical values, and applies the exact four-column `NOCASE` predicate to count and page queries. Tests exercise each source column, case-insensitive matching, and a `%_\` input against a wildcard-shaped control row to prove that all three metacharacters remain literal.

**Validation and limits.** This is bounded **RECOVERED static** analysis plus **IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED** SQLite behavior. `LWB-R7-075` now supersedes the City live-evidence gap: a current-v19 Fast scan published/reopened 1,546 City rows, and exact raw-record set comparison passed positive identity keyword plus literal `%`, `_` and `\\` inputs before and after reopen. Alliance/no-alliance and positive `markedOnly` were proven in the same checkpoint; the mark existed only in the disposable proof database, with no game or persistent user mark state changed. Ordinary quality, completion clocks and per-kind plunderability were separate findings later closed where applicable; other still-unproven per-kind live filters remain tracked in the active R7 audit.

### LWB-R6-008 — consistent count/page SQLite snapshot (2026-09-08)

**IMPLEMENTATION POLICY source and rationale.** Project-manager review 4 identified that `MapDataStore.SearchIndexed` issued its recovered count and page SQL as separate reads protected only by the store instance's process-local lock. A second SQLite connection could therefore publish rows between the count and page queries. The original LWBridge transaction strategy for `map_search` has not been recovered, so this checkpoint does **not** claim an original transaction mode. The rebuild policy is that one `map_search` response must describe one database generation. The implementation uses Microsoft.Data.Sqlite `10.0.11` `BeginTransaction(deferred: true)`; the inspected package assembly SHA-256 is `4abd9c2a61e580eb853e93ca8953a3cef2c05714ae28d2d1859d4dbc5e5700bc`. The verified LWBridge reference remains SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`, but no new original behavior is inferred from it for this policy.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapDataStore.SearchIndexedCore` now starts one deferred SQLite transaction before the count query and assigns the same transaction to both count and page commands. A deterministic file-backed regression opens a second `MapDataStore` connection in WAL mode, commits a third city row after the first connection has observed a count of two, and then verifies that the in-flight search still returns `total=2` with two pre-existing rows. After the read transaction closes, the same database reports all three rows, proving that the writer committed without changing the search snapshot.

**Reproduction and limits.** Run `dotnet run --project tests\LWBridge.Desktop.Checks\LWBridge.Desktop.Checks.csproj --configuration Release`; the deterministic result reports `mapContract=true`. Durable checkpoint metadata is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-08-r6-map-search-snapshot.json`. This is **IMPLEMENTATION POLICY** plus **IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED** behavior, not **RECOVERED** original transaction semantics and not **LIVE-PROVEN** scan ingestion. Advanced filters/sorts, `map_data_options`, authoritative summary state, export and per-kind native keys remain separately gated.

### LWB-R6-009 — option SQL family, treasure selector and dispatch-level filters (2026-09-08)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The verified reference remains `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`; the correlated recovered frontend `MapDataPanel-C1HVeNHr.js` remains SHA-256 `fc39de8154c54ee0937b6f9f9377d671e0dbad533ed2d9eda29a99d35e380f5e`. Bounded raw-PE printable-string inspection records the option SQL family at `0x00C83048-0x00C83A72`, the latest server scan-summary query at `0x00C849FF-0x00C84AC5`, level predicates around `0x00C89BE4-0x00C89C1A`, treasure predicates at `0x00C89D3E-0x00C89F37`, and adjacent derived-sort strings at `0x00C8A918-0x00C8ABEB`. The durable focused excerpt and metadata are `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-08-r6-map-options-advanced-filters.txt` and `.json`.

**RECOVERED result.** The option producer exposes city alliance counts ordered `COLLATE NOCASE`, resource/monster name-key counts grouped by kind/name, distinct dispatch levels `>=1` ordered ascending, and treasure option grouping that deliberately normalizes exactly one dimension: `suppliesType>0` produces `supplies_type` with `treasure_type=0`, while ordinary treasure rows produce `treasure_type` with `supplies_type=0`. The reward-item option query groups truck/railway `currentGoods` `{key,name,iconPath}` and excludes expired arrivals through `arriveTs > ?`; its cutoff producer/unit was unrecovered at R6-009 and is closed later by `LWB-R6-014`. `scanProgress` reads the newest non-`discarded` `scan_runs` row for the selected server using `ORDER BY updated_at DESC LIMIT 1`.

The persisted-search builder separately exposes exact treasure predicates `CAST(json_extract(data_json,'$.suppliesType') AS INTEGER) = ?`, `CAST(json_extract(data_json,'$.treasureType') AS INTEGER) = ?`, and the ordinary-treasure guard `COALESCE(CAST(json_extract(data_json,'$.suppliesType') AS INTEGER),0) = 0`. Dispatch level filtering uses `level >= ?` and `level <= ?`; the recovered frontend emits the same selected dispatch level into `minLevel` and `maxLevel`.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapDataQueryContract` now accepts only the recovered treasure-selection shape: both numeric dimensions are present, exactly one is positive and the other is zero. `MapDataStore.SearchIndexed` applies the corresponding supplies predicate or ordinary treasure predicate plus the recovered zero guard. Dispatch positive `minLevel`/`maxLevel` values are also admitted for the recovered dispatch kind and applied to both count and page SQL. Deterministic rows prove ordinary treasure versus supplies isolation, reject partial/both-positive treasure selections, and prove an exact dispatch-level result.

**Validation and limits.** `dotnet run --project tests\LWBridge.Desktop.Checks\LWBridge.Desktop.Checks.csproj --configuration Release` reports `mapContract=true`. A convenience executable verifier attempted after the successful bounded byte inspection was rejected by the active environment's automatic safety review; it was not retried or routed through another executor. The saved excerpt records only observations already obtained before that rejection. At R6-009, `map_data_options` remained fail-closed on the reward cutoff clock and the completion/plunderability mappings were still open; `LWB-R6-013/014` later close quality plus the recovered time-dependent public search forms and reward cutoff. Foreign-radar/lucky treasure behavior, authoritative `map_summary` server selection, public options source/run-context/full assembly and remaining non-`updatedAt` sort mapping remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. This finding is static plus offline SQLite proof, not native-ingestion or live-game proof.

### LWB-R6-010 — quality selector kind gates and railway reindeer correction (2026-09-08)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The immutable recovered `MapDataPanel-C1HVeNHr.js` remains SHA-256 `fc39de8154c54ee0937b6f9f9377d671e0dbad533ed2d9eda29a99d35e380f5e`. Character offset `5977` defines quality-capable kinds as `truck`, `railway`, `dispatch` and `ghost`; offset `9155` maps numeric display quality `1=N`, `2=R`, `3=SR`, `4=SSR`, and every value `>=5` to `UR`. The query builder at offsets `37243`/`37279` omits ordinary `quality` when the selected value is `special` or `reindeer`, emitting `specialOnly`/`reindeerOnly` instead. The visible selector around `52301` offers ordinary `n/r/sr/ssr/ur`, `special` only for dispatch/ghost, and at offset `52723` the `reindeer` option is explicitly guarded by `F===truck`. Durable evidence is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-08-r6-map-quality-selector-kind-gates.txt` and `.json`.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED correction.** `MapDataQueryContract` now accepts `reindeerOnly=true` only for truck. Railway `reindeerOnly=true` returns to `MAP_QUERY_UNRECOVERED`, and a deterministic regression pins that fail-closed behavior. The already recovered binary predicate `CAST(json_extract(data_json,'$.isSpecialURQuality') AS INTEGER) = 1` remains unchanged.

**Validation and limits.** This corrects an overbroad frontend-kind interpretation from `LWB-R6-006`; it does not alter the recovered predicate itself. Ordinary quality filtering remains **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED** because the frontend display mapping proves `UR` covers all numeric qualities `>=5`, while the exact original backend filter/range binding is still unrecovered. The rebuild therefore must not guess `ur` as either equality to `5` or `>=5`. This is recovered frontend evidence plus offline contract verification, not a live-game query proof.

### LWB-R6-013 — exact ordinary-quality mapping and truck UR exclusion (2026-09-08)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe` remains SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. `tools/inspect_lwbridge_map_quality.py` verifies the hash, the map-search function at preferred-image VA `0x140271864`-`0x140276D7B`, the `quality` raw marker `0x00C89E59` / xref `0x140273E02`, the shared equality helper `0x14027A129`-`0x14027A1E3`, literal UR SQL assembly at `0x140273FCD`/`0x140273FDA`, and the truck+UR guard xref `0x140274093`. Durable evidence is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-08-r6-map-quality-filter.txt` and `.json`.

**RECOVERED result.** The frontend-emitted ordinary selector strings map exactly as follows: `n -> quality = ?` with parameter `1`; `r -> quality = ?` with `2`; `sr -> quality = ?` with `3`; `ssr -> quality = ?` with `4`; `ur -> quality >= 5` with no quality parameter. The shared helper constructs `quality = ?` and binds the integer passed from the selector branch. This resolves the prior UR uncertainty as `>=5`, not equality to `5`.

For ordinary truck `ur` only, the original additionally appends `COALESCE(CAST(json_extract(data_json,'$.isSpecialURQuality') AS INTEGER),0) = 0`, excluding the special-UR/reindeer rows selected separately by `reindeerOnly`. The kind and selector checks are explicit at `0x140274033`-`0x140274078`; railway/dispatch/ghost UR do not receive this additional predicate from that branch. This refines `LWB-R6-012` without deleting its earlier bounded adjacency evidence.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapDataQueryContract` now accepts exactly `n/r/sr/ssr/ur` for the four recovered quality-capable kinds (`truck`, `railway`, `dispatch`, `ghost`). `MapDataStore.SearchIndexed` applies equality with parameters `1/2/3/4`, literal `quality >= 5` for UR, and the additional special-quality-zero guard only for truck UR. Deterministic truck rows cover the equality selectors, a quality-5 ordinary UR row, a non-special quality-7 UR row, a quality-6 `isSpecialURQuality=true` row excluded from ordinary truck UR, and a quality-8 row on another server. Page-size-one UR searches prove `{rows,total}` stays consistent at total two for server 7 and excludes the server-8 row. Persisted railway/dispatch/ghost UR rows carry `isSpecialURQuality=true` and remain selected, proving the exclusion is truck-only; railway additionally combines UR with the recovered retained-item predicate. Numeric quality, unknown strings and mismatched kinds stay fail-closed.

**Validation and limits.** Reproduce with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_quality.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe --json`, `dotnet build src\LWBridge.Desktop\LWBridge.Desktop.csproj --configuration Release`, and `dotnet run --project tests\LWBridge.Desktop.Checks\LWBridge.Desktop.Checks.csproj --configuration Release`. The build succeeds with zero warnings/errors and the deterministic suite reports `ok=true`, `mapContract=true`. Implementation metadata is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-08-r6-map-quality-implementation.json`. The original backend also contains a numeric quality input path that binds equality, but the recovered frontend emits the five string forms above; the rebuild therefore does not broaden its public recovered-UI contract. At R6-013 the completion/plunder clock slice was still open; `LWB-R6-014` below closes the recovered frontend forms. Treasure visibility/lucky behavior and non-`updatedAt` sorts remain separate DB-05 gaps. Static and offline verification are not **LIVE-PROVEN** query/ingestion behavior.

### LWB-R6-014 — precise wall clock, completion status and plunderability (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The verified reference remains `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. `tools/inspect_lwbridge_map_time_filters.py` verifies `map_search` at preferred-image VA `0x140271864-0x140276D7B`, the shared clock helper `0x14023F1C0-0x14023F244`, and import-table slot `0x1407B94B0 = kernel32.dll!GetSystemTimePreciseAsFileTime`. The helper subtracts FILETIME epoch `116444736000000000` 100-ns ticks and returns `max(FILETIME-epoch,0) // 10000`, i.e. Unix milliseconds. `map_search` samples it once at `0x140272353`; reward-option aggregation samples the same helper at `0x14025A1B5`. Durable evidence is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-map-time-filters.json`.

**RECOVERED result.** Truck and railway always append `(arriveTs IS NULL OR arriveTs > now)` before optional plunderability. Their frontend-emitted `plunderableOnly=true` additionally requires non-null `arriveTs` and positive remaining loot, using the recovered fallback `MAX(maxLootCount-robTimes,0)`. The native dispatch/ghost/treasure plunder branch requires positive `completionTime`, positive `plunderAt` falling back to `completionTime`, nonexpired `taskExpireTime`, and remaining steal capacity; the recovered frontend emits `plunderableOnly` only for truck/railway/dispatch, so the rebuild does not expose the wider native branch.

Dispatch/ghost `completionStatus="pending"` means `completionTime IS NULL OR <=0 OR > now`; `"completed"` means `completionTime >0 AND <= now`. Both bind the same sampled Unix-ms clock. R6-014 also closes R6-009's reward-item cutoff gap: reward options use this same clock for their recovered `arriveTs > ?` predicate.

**IMPLEMENTATION boundary.** `MapDataQueryContract` now accepts only frontend-emitted `completionStatus` values (`pending`/`completed`) for dispatch/ghost and `plunderableOnly=true` for truck/railway/dispatch. `MapDataStore.SearchIndexed` applies the exact recovered SQL shapes and one captured time value to count and page queries; truck/railway active-arrival filtering also applies when `plunderableOnly` is absent. `RecoveredWallClock` mirrors the recovered Windows clock source and conversion. Unsupported false/mismatched/unknown forms remain fail-closed through `MAP_QUERY_UNRECOVERED`.

**LIVE acceptance — `LWB-R7-073`, 2026-09-20.** Current-v19 Dispatch now proves the recovered filter family against real committed data rather than fixtures alone. A Fast full-world scan published/reopened 69 Dispatch rows. At one frozen Unix-ms clock, the verifier independently derived expected identity sets from row fields and required exact equality for N/R/SR/SSR/UR quality, `specialOnly`, pending/completed `completionStatus`, `minLevel`, `maxLevel`, exact-level `minLevel+maxLevel`, and `plunderableOnly`; all 12 checks repeated identically after database reopen. The live population contained positive SR/SSR/UR, special, pending/completed, level and plunderable branches, while N/R correctly produced empty sets. The same helper is ready for Ghost when its owner-deferred positive population can be tested. A contemporaneous Railway run on server 2204 had zero rows, so no Railway filter acceptance was inferred from the earlier R7-071 population.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED validation and limits.** Reproduce static evidence with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_time_filters.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe --json` and frontend kind gates with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_frontend_consumers.py evidence\lwbridge-0.3.1\frontend --json`. `python tools\build_lwbridge_frontend.py --check`, the Release desktop build, `dotnet run --project tests\LWBridge.Desktop.Checks\LWBridge.Desktop.Checks.csproj --configuration Release -- --verify-real-config-unchanged`, and the preference-provider/live-transport/transport-boundary Node checks all pass. The deterministic SQLite cases pin strict `arriveTs > now`, direct/fallback remaining loot, pending/completed equality boundaries, dispatch expiry `> now`, steal capacity and fail-closed unsupported forms. This is **RECOVERED static** plus **IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED**, not **LIVE-PROVEN** ingestion or gameplay behavior. Treasure foreign-radar/viewer/lucky behavior, alternate sorts, public options source/run-context/full response assembly and authoritative summary-server selection remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**.

### LWB-R7-058 — City export default filename and UTC clock (2026-09-19)

**RECOVERED static.** `tools/inspect_lwbridge_city_export_filename.py` hash-locks the verified original `lwbridge-0.3.1.exe` and decodes the Rust format-argument bytecode at raw `0x00821F12` using the exact embedded rustc commit `4a4ef493e3a1488c6e321570238084b38948f6db`. The bytecode resolves to literal `map-cities-`, one default decimal placeholder, literal `-`, then six placeholders with flags `0x69000020` and widths `4,2,2,2,2,2`, literal `-` between date and time, and `.xlsx`. The compiler source for that exact rustc commit defines bit 24 as zero-padding and bit 27 as width-present, so the six date/time fields are fixed-width zero-padded. The adjacent original Feedback export has the identical six-field suffix without the leading server placeholder, corroborating the timestamp structure.

**Argument/clock ownership.** The bounded picker path reads exact key `serverId`, stores the positive value in the first formatter slot, then calls clock helper `RVA 0x10A30B`. That helper imports `kernel32!GetSystemTimePreciseAsFileTime`, subtracts the Windows FILETIME-to-Unix epoch constant `116444736000000000`, builds the embedded `time 0.3.54` representation and stores a zero UTC offset. `time 0.3.54` source matches the observed extraction: `Date.value >> 10` yields calendar year; ordinal/leap-year arithmetic yields month/day; little-endian `Time` packs second/minute/hour at bits 32/40/48 and the caller extracts those bytes. The exact recovered default filename is therefore `map-cities-{serverId}-{YYYY}{MM}{DD}-{HH}{mm}{ss}.xlsx` in UTC.

**Restriction/remaining gate.** One later narrow request to inspect an earlier RSI assignment was automatically rejected as **SB-99**. It was not replayed or rerouted and is not used by the verifier/evidence. Filename/timestamp is no longer an export blocker. At this checkpoint original internal pagination/scope, direct A-C/J typing/coercion, default save directory, complete `rfd` cancel/error/existing-destination behavior and original large-ID workbook typing/reopen behavior remained open; R7-059 below subsequently closes the save-dialog/default-location/cancel/overwrite slice. Evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-19-r7-city-export-filename.json`.

### LWB-R7-060 — City export full row/data contract and public enablement (2026-09-19)

**RECOVERED static + IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** Hash-locked Stage 1 (`RVA 0x334604-0x334A06`) proves the original host does not export only the frontend's first 200 rows. It overwrites the City query with page 1/pageSize 200, appends returned `rows`, stops on an empty page or when accumulated rows reach returned `total`, and advances through page 1000. If page 1000 is still nonempty while accumulated rows remain below total, it emits exact `MAP_EXPORT_FAILED / city export exceeded the row limit`. The original effective ceiling is therefore 200,000 rows. The rebuild keeps one SQLite read snapshot for consistency and enforces the same ceiling before materializing rows.

Hash-locked Stage 2 (`RVA 0x335B41-0x3374AD`) closes the exact workbook row contract: A=`serverId`, B=`x`, C=`y`, D=`ownerName`, E=`ownerUid`, F=`uuid`, G=`allianceName`, H=`level`, I=`health`, J=`protectEndTime` with `shieldEndTime` fallback only when the primary key is absent, K localized marked yes/no, L=`updatedAt`. A/B/C/H/I use numeric cells. D/E/F/G/K use escaped `inlineStr`; the strict field extractor accepts only JSON String, proving large UID/UUID preservation is original behavior. J/L use style-3 datetime cells and accept only positive finite JSON Number. The exact unit rule is `<1e11 => seconds * 1000`, otherwise milliseconds, followed by `ms / 86400000 + 25569`. Marked uses the yes label only for literal JSON `true`.

The success branch is also exact: `{canceled:false,path:<selected path>,rowCount:<actual written count>}`. Together with R7-059's exact cancel/error-collapse result, R7-058's UTC filename and R6-018/R7-057 package/layout recovery, this closes the City export contract. Production `map_city_export` is now intercepted on the WinForms UI thread for a native save dialog with no app-set directory/title and `Excel workbook|*.xlsx`; full-filter snapshot/workbook generation runs off the UI thread. Direct backend invocation stays `NATIVE_DIALOG_REQUIRED` so dialog ownership cannot be bypassed. Deterministic acceptance reopens a 235-row workbook, preserves a 36-digit UID, proves strict coercion, J fallback and the 1e11 boundary; the full deterministic suite passes. No interactive user save was performed. Evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-19-r7-city-export-row-contract.json`.

### LWB-R7-059 — City export save-dialog/default-location/cancel contract (2026-09-19)

**RECOVERED static + immutable upstream-source corroboration.** `tools/inspect_lwbridge_city_export_dialog.py` hash-locks the original executable and verifies the recovered rfd 0.16.0 callsite. The binary contains the exact Windows CID provenance string for `rfd-0.16.0`; upstream tag `0.16.0` resolves to commit `5d32eec3a7930eb43b7e864eb773831bbd3d91b4`, with Windows save wrapper blob `d5c414a87a6f42416b2c63c7b53a5eeaa079ae7e` and `dialog_ffi.rs` blob `29e9dd330259db60a88569c64528ef7c5a3b5485`.

The City builder starts with `starting_directory=None`, `file_name=None`, and `title=None`; the City path adds exactly one filter, display name `Excel workbook`, extension `xlsx`, and replaces only `file_name` with the R7-058 value before synchronous `save_file()`. The recovered builder offsets are consistent with rfd's `filters`, `starting_directory`, `file_name`, `title` sequence. No City-path write populates `starting_directory` or title. In rfd's Windows backend `set_path(None)` returns without calling `SetFolder`, so there is **no application-selected default folder**; the Windows Common Item Dialog uses its remembered/system location. `Excel workbook` is therefore refined from R6-018's generic “dialog label” wording to the filter display name, while the dialog title remains the Windows default.

The first `xlsx` filter is also passed to `SetDefaultExtension` and `SetFileTypes`. `build_save_file` does not call `SetOptions`; Windows Save dialogs therefore retain their documented defaults including `FOS_OVERWRITEPROMPT`, so choosing an existing path uses the standard native overwrite confirmation. rfd's synchronous `FileSaveDialogImpl::save_file` ends with `run(self).ok()`: user cancellation and any COM/build/show/get-result error collapse identically to `Option::None` before returning to LWBridge.

**Exact None result.** The verified original embeds `serde_json-1.0.151`; its `Value` order is `Null`, `Bool`, `Number`, `String`, `Array`, `Object`. The LWBridge `None` branch writes tag/payload `0x0101` for `Bool(true)` under `canceled`, constructs a tag-3 empty `String` for `path`, and writes tag-2 zero payload for `rowCount`. The exact result is `{canceled:true,path:"",rowCount:0}`. Dialog failure is therefore intentionally indistinguishable from user cancel at this public boundary and is not surfaced as `MAP_EXPORT_FAILED` by rfd.

**Historical remaining gate at R7-059.** Picker/default-location/cancel/overwrite semantics were no longer blockers here; the row/data contract was still incomplete at this checkpoint. R7-060 subsequently recovers pagination/scope, A-L typing/coercion and large-ID behavior and enables the public command. SB-08/SB-09/SB-99 remain preserved and were not replayed. Evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-19-r7-city-export-dialog.json`.

The helper is internal/test-only and deliberately does not interpret status values, decide retry eligibility, schedule blocks, acknowledge native capture or publish staged rows. Deterministic coverage proves a second write to the same run/block replaces the prior checkpoint rather than creating a duplicate, the latest payload/status/attempt/error values survive a file-backed database reopen, and `ClearServer` removes the owning `scan_run` so SQLite's recovered cascade removes its block checkpoints.

The Release desktop build succeeds with zero warnings/errors, and `LWBridge.Desktop.Checks` returns `ok=true` with `profileRouting`, `persistence`, `requestLifetime`, `mapPersistence` and `mapContract` all true. This gives a durable storage primitive needed by eventual interrupted-scan resume/reconciliation without weakening the production scan gate. It enables no live/public Map Data operation.

**Limits and current research state.** Original block status vocabulary/transitions, scheduling/concurrency ownership, acknowledgement timing, retry counts/eligibility, inflight treatment after bridge loss, completion/publication eligibility and per-kind native `record_key` derivation remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. The independent runtime-target check during this checkpoint found no running LastWar/LWBridge process, so no supported active-scan observation was available. ### LWB-R6-023 — exact treasure option key formatting (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The source remains the verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. Rather than repeat the denied whole-executable descriptor-xref route, the enhanced `tools/inspect_lwbridge_map_treasure_option_keys.py` uses pefile 2024.8.26 and Capstone 5.0.6 to disassemble only the already identified option-assembly function at preferred-image VA `0x140257AA4-0x14025B436`. Both prefixes are length-prefixed descriptors: raw `0x00C83B20` is byte `9` followed by `treasure:` and raw `0x00C83B58` is byte `9` followed by `supplies:`. The function references those descriptors directly at `0x140259E4B` and `0x140259C11` respectively. The treasure branch's adjacent 12-byte field literal at VA `0x140838A85` is verified as `treasureType`.

**RECOVERED data flow and result.** The recovered R6-009 SQL returns normalized `supplies_type` first and `treasure_type` second, with exactly one positive for every emitted option. The native row path loads those first two numeric values from `[rsp+0x50]` and `[rsp+0x58]`, copies them to `[rsp+0x200]` and `[rsp+0x250]`, then tests the first value at `0x140259B9D`. A positive first value enters the supplies branch, which formats `[rsp+0x200]` after the `supplies:` descriptor and emits the `suppliesType` field. Otherwise the treasure branch formats `[rsp+0x250]` after the `treasure:` descriptor and emits the verified `treasureType` field. Both paths use formatter `0x1400397B0`; its numeric helper `0x1400394C0-0x14003968E` performs signed base-10 integer conversion using decimal divisions/digit-table output. Because the SQL emission guard requires the selected dimension to be positive, the resulting option keys are exactly `supplies:<positive decimal suppliesType>` and `treasure:<positive decimal treasureType>`.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** The existing persisted-only `ReadPersistedOptionAggregatesAtForTest` helper now carries the recovered `key` alongside each treasure option. It uses invariant decimal rendering and the recovered positive-dimension branch; deterministic coverage uses multi-digit `treasureType=12` and `suppliesType=4`, expecting `treasure:12` and `supplies:4`. `git diff --check` and the hash-locked inspector pass; the Release build completes with zero warnings/errors and the deterministic backend suite returns `ok=true`, `mapPersistence=true` and `mapContract=true`. This extends the R6-015 test kernel only. Public `map_data_options` remains fail-closed because original persisted-vs-active-run source selection, exact native count/no-alliance/full response assembly and other source-dependent semantics are still unrecovered. Detailed native evidence is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-map-treasure-option-descriptors.json`; compact implementation/validation metadata is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-map-treasure-option-key-implementation.json`.

### LWB-R6-025 — native map summary data flow and fail-closed boundary (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The reference remains `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. The hash-locked `tools/inspect_lwbridge_map_scan.py` identifies the `map_summary` future at preferred-image VA `0x140151509-0x140152388`. It calls the shared map-scan state producer `0x1400F7F47` at `0x140151A26`, looks up the eight-byte `serverId` field at `0x140151AFD`, and calls the native map-count helper `0x140271056` at `0x140151BC0`. The successful response path allocates the keys `serverId` at `0x140151C59`, `counts` at `0x140151CD6`, and `scanState` at `0x140151D74`.

**RECOVERED data flow.** The successful shared scan-state result is copied into an owned local value and later serialized under `scanState`. The `serverId` lookup result is held in `rsi`; immediately before the count call it is passed in `r8`, and the count helper prologue moves that value into its server parameter. The response assembly later serializes the same `rsi` value under `serverId`. The count helper independently proves the R6-024 source abstraction at control-flow level: if either optional run pointer or length is absent it chooses `map_records` with `server_id=?1`; when both are present it chooses `scan_records` with `server_id=?1 AND run_id=?2`. `map_summary` receives that pointer/length from an internal value produced just after `scanState.serverId` extraction. Therefore the original successful envelope is exactly `{serverId, counts, scanState}`, and the server used for counts is the server exposed by the shared scan state. The exact internal rule that supplies or omits the run scope is still **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**.

**IMPLEMENTATION POLICY / OFFLINE-TESTED boundary (historical; superseded for current public summary).** At R6-025 the production rebuild removed an earlier synthetic success object and rejected with `MAP_INDEX_UNAVAILABLE` because the run-scope selector and scan-state integration were still unrecovered. R6-030/R6-033/R6-036/R6-037 and later R7 integration subsequently closed those prerequisites. `LWB-R7-078` now LIVE-PROVES current-v19 public `map_summary`: the exact `{serverId,counts,scanState}` envelope exposes the owned active Monster run, exact persisted counts after completion, and the unique saved-profile server after database reopen while keeping saved context explicitly non-live (`phase=unavailable`, `serverIdSource=saved_profile_index`). The historical R6 fail-closed statement no longer describes current production.

None was retried or rerouted. The successful bounded function dumps above are sufficient for the stated envelope/server/count flow, but not for the semantic condition inside helper `0x140344F1D`, its run identity source, complete unavailable/error behavior, or live current-client proof. ### LWB-R6-030 — native options/summary run-scope selector (2026-09-09)

### LWB-R6-031 — native option response/no-alliance/scan-progress selection (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED source identity.** The reference remains `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. This checkpoint used only bounded reads of the already identified option function `0x140257AA4-0x14025B436`, its small alliance row/text decoders, and the two scan-run helpers. It did not replay any historical denied whole-executable xref operation. Durable locators and reproduction commands are in `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-map-option-response-assembly.json`.

**RECOVERED `noAllianceCount`.** R6-009's native alliance SQL returns `alliance_name,COUNT(*)` grouped by name. Row decoder `0x140287B25-0x140287C59` reads column 0 as nullable text and column 1 as the count. Its nullable-text path canonicalizes SQL NULL to the same zero-length representation tested by the caller. The option loop initializes a zero accumulator at `0x140258728`; after row decode it loads the name length and grouped count, and `0x14025894A-0x14025894D` branches on zero length. Nonempty names build `{name,count}` and enter `alliances[]`; zero-length names reach `0x140258AE4`, which adds the grouped count to the accumulator. `0x14025ACBC` later loads that accumulator and inserts it under `noAllianceCount`. Therefore NULL and empty alliance groups are both omitted from `alliances[]` and summed into `noAllianceCount`; no separate no-alliance SQL query is used by the original path.

**RECOVERED top-level assembly order.** A bounded RIP-xref pass inside the same option function maps the contiguous result keys to insertion sites in this exact sequence: `serverId` (`0x14025A9C0`), `counts` (`0x14025AA1D`), `alliances` (`0x14025AB2B`), `names` (`0x14025ABA7`), `dispatchLevels` (`0x14025AC23`), `noAllianceCount` (`0x14025AC9F`), `rewardItems` (`0x14025AD09`), `treasureTypes` (`0x14025AD85`), `scanProgress` (`0x14025AE01`). The `counts` insertion immediately follows the already recovered source/scope count-helper call at `0x14025AA62`.

**RECOVERED scan-progress record selection.** The option prologue tests whether the optional run pointer and length are present. On the no-run/persisted branch, `0x14025AE32` calls helper `0x14025EAEC`; that helper directly references the R6-009 SQL selecting the newest non-`discarded` `scan_runs` row for `server_id=?1` and the diagnostic `read latest scan summary`. On the active-run branch, `0x14025B06E-0x14025B079` calls helper `0x140276D7B` with the exact run pointer/length preserved from R6-030; that helper directly references `SELECT id,server_id,selected_types,status,total_blocks,completed_blocks,failed_blocks,created_at,updated_at,error FROM scan_runs WHERE id=?1`. Thus public options use newest non-discarded server progress when persisted and the exact active `scanRunId` when staging.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED boundary.** The existing persisted-only option kernel now mirrors the recovered alliance/no-alliance behavior: NULL is normalized to an empty name, zero-length alliance groups are summed into `noAllianceCount`, only nonempty names enter `alliances[]`, and the former separate implementation-policy no-alliance `COUNT(*)` query is removed. Deterministic tests verify the two no-alliance groups contribute `2` while only `Alpha` remains in the alliance list. Production `map_data_options` is still unchanged and fail-closed.

**Remaining gate.** The common post-query conversion after `0x14025B07E` still needs field-level recovery to prove the exact `scanProgress` JSON value, including its complete fields and precise missing/null/error representation. That is now the principal ESC-003 options-assembly gap. Complete summary unavailable/error behavior and live native ingestion remain separate. This checkpoint enables no public UI operation.

### LWB-R6-032 — scan-progress serializer edge and restricted row mapper (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED source identity and post-query edge.** The reference remains `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. A bounded read of `0x1402AD389-0x1402AD510` confirms that helper dispatches by a generic value tag and handles multiple value classes; it is not the scan-run-specific field mapper. Therefore the exact public field mapping occurs earlier in the query result path and must not be inferred from the ten SQL columns alone.

**RECOVERED persisted row-mapper locator.** In latest-scan helper `0x14025EAEC-0x14025ED53`, the query setup stores closure/vtable address `0x140C81A30` immediately before issuing the exact R6-031 ten-column `scan_runs` query. The immutable vtable data at raw `0x00C80E30` contains qwords `0`, `8`, `8`, `0x140294188`, identifying `0x140294188` as the persisted query's row-mapper callback. The nearby raw field literals `totalBlocks` (`0x00C84B48`), `completedBlocks` (`0x00C84B70`), `failedBlocks` (`0x00C84B98`) and `updatedAt` (`0x00C84BD8`) are recorded only as adjacency evidence; without callback control flow they are not promoted to proven `scanProgress` membership/order.

**RECOVERED metadata separation.** Printable metadata around raw `0x008261A8-0x0082672D` and `0x00C99D1F-0x00C99FC8` belongs to the broader `src\services\map_scan.rs` live scan-state/event contract and includes fields such as `scanRunId`, read/unread/inflight counts, scan rate, native pending/dropped records and capture readiness. The public options frontend only consumes `scanProgress.serverId/id/status/createdAt/updatedAt/error`. The broader live-state vocabulary is therefore not used as a substitute for the still-unrecovered persisted `scanProgress` serializer.

**Restrictions and remaining gate.** SB-32 through SB-35 record automatic-review rejections for a helper-bound lookup, direct bounded disassembly of `0x140294188`, and two raw-region inspection attempts. None was repeated or rerouted and none contributes field-mapping evidence. Exact JSON keys/order, `selected_types` conversion, optional progress-count fields, absent-row/null behavior and query/conversion error propagation remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. `map_data_options` remains fail-closed. ### LWB-R6-033 — native `map_summary` failure propagation (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The reference remains `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. The hash-locked bounded inspector reproduces `map_summary` at preferred-image VA `0x140151509-0x140152388` and the shared scan-state producer at `0x1400F7F47-0x1400F9333`. After the producer call at `0x140151A26`, the ready-result discriminant at `0x140151AC8-0x140151ACE` sends the non-success path to `0x140151B55-0x140151BA5`. That branch preserves the returned error payload and joins the command's common completion path at `0x140151ED4`.

**RECOVERED propagation behavior.** A shared scan-state failure bypasses the `serverId` lookup at `0x140151B0D`, optional run-scope producer at `0x140151B26`, count helper at `0x140151BC0` and every successful response-key insertion. The count helper has the same command-level rule: after its result at `0x140151BC5`, the non-success branch reaches `0x140151E5A`, preserves the count error and joins the same completion path without assembling `{serverId,counts,scanState}`. Therefore the original successful envelope is emitted only after both prerequisites succeed; internal scan-state/count failure is propagated rather than replaced with an empty or zero-filled summary.

**Boundary and implementation impact (historical; current integration superseded).** R6-033 closed the orchestration-level propagation rule but did not assign exact original error codes to shared-producer conditions; the verified binary vocabulary therefore remained deliberately unassigned by branch. Later R6/R7 work integrated authoritative scan-state/current-server sources without inventing those original mappings. `LWB-R7-078` now LIVE-PROVES the current public success path on v19 across active, completed and reopened saved-context states, while existing deterministic cases retain unavailable/ambiguous/failure branches. Durable historical evidence remains `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-map-summary-error-propagation.json`; SB-36 through SB-40 remain rejected attempts that contributed no technical evidence.

**RECOVERED current-client source identity and tooling.** The current official managed artifact remains build `1.0.361 / 1078` `Assembly-CSharp.rdl`, size `15,640,576`, SHA-256 `871efe06819fbac438413eb96b7df8193d0be56094f3a44d5ff141e6219adcbd`. ### LWB-R6-035 — current-client `DCPlayer` owner/access chain (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED managed ownership.** The source remains the exact build `1.0.361 / 1078` `Assembly-CSharp.rdl`, SHA-256 `871efe06819fbac438413eb96b7df8193d0be56094f3a44d5ff141e6219adcbd`. The R6-034 read-only inspector now adds `--references-to-type`, which reproduces field row `2447` as `CustomDataManager GameEntry::<Data>k__BackingField` and field row `3534` as `DCPlayer CustomDataManager::<Player>k__BackingField`. `GameEntry::get_Data()` is method row `3736`, RVA `0x502ee`, and its body is exactly `ldsfld token(0x679E83C2); ret`, proving `GameEntry.Data` is static. `CustomDataManager::get_Player()` is method row `5419`, RVA `0xa6fb2`, and reads instance field token `0x679E3B22`, proving the data manager owns the `DCPlayer` reference. Combined with R6-034 method row `3426`, the current managed access sequence is `GameEntry.Data -> CustomDataManager.Player -> DCPlayer.GetCurServerId()`.

**RECOVERED xLua exposure.** Generated wrappers expose every hop: `GameEntryWrap::_g_get_Data` is row `24694` / RVA `0x24b4d0`; `CustomDataManagerWrap::_g_get_Player` is row `22825` / RVA `0x20dea4`; and `DCPlayerWrap::_m_GetCurServerId` is row `22900` / RVA `0x2102ac`. This establishes a current-client xLua-visible property/method sequence without guessing the exact LWBridge script spelling. `GameEntry::Init()` also executes `newobj token(0xA79D3F62)` at IL `0x005e`; `CustomDataManagerWrap::__CreateInstance` independently uses the same constructor token. The protected call immediately after the `GameEntry.Init` construction remains unresolved, so this finding does not claim exact assignment timing or readiness from that sequence alone.

**Boundary and implementation impact.** This closes R6-034's managed state-owner/access-path subproblem, but it does not establish how LWBridge obtains or invokes that sequence, when `Data`/`Player` are guaranteed non-null in the bridge lifecycle, or which original error is returned when those prerequisites are unavailable. No production host/backend code changes and no public command are enabled. `map_summary` and `map_scan_status` remain fail-closed until the bridge-facing producer/readiness/error contract is source-attributed or live-proven. No Daybreak request is warranted because the permitted current-RDL route produced positive evidence. Durable evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-current-server-access-chain.json`.

**Validation after interrupted-session resume.** `python -m py_compile tools\inspect_lastwar_rdl_metadata.py`, evidence-JSON parsing/assertions, `python tools\build_lwbridge_frontend.py --check`, the Release desktop build and the deterministic backend suite with `--verify-real-config-unchanged` all pass. The installed diagnostic reports a valid x64 client and no running game/launcher. SB-46 and SB-47 record two rejected combined read/validation commands; neither supplied technical evidence and neither was rerouted. The exact installed-RDL reproduction commands were therefore not replayed during the resumed validation; the R6-035 source facts remain the already captured current-build static evidence, not live proof.

### LWB-R6-036 — shared scan-state producer bridge request/normalization slice (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED bridge request order.** The source remains verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. The already identified shared producer is preferred-image VA `0x1400F7F47-0x1400F9333`. Its first bridge request installs command `getWorldMapState` at `0x1400F7FBE`, length `16`, a `5,000 ms` timeout at `0x1400F7FD7`, then calls common dispatcher `0x1400E4FAD` at `0x1400F7FFF`. Its next bridge request installs `getCurrentServerId` at `0x1400F80B9`, length `18`, the same `5,000 ms` timeout at `0x1400F80D2`, and calls the same dispatcher at `0x1400F80F7`. This closes the native producer's request order without asserting how either command is implemented inside the current client.

**RECOVERED normalization slice.** The `getCurrentServerId` result is queried for `serverId` at `0x1400F81BE-0x1400F81D3`. The saved world-map-state value is queried for `isInWorld` at `0x1400F8225-0x1400F8232`; missing or non-boolean values branch to `0x1400F8325-0x1400F8333`, which normalizes the emitted value to false. `homeServerId` is queried at `0x1400F835F-0x1400F8374` and inserted only when positive. `seasonServerIds` and `truckMatchServerIds` are decoded from array values, accept only numeric IDs `1..99999`, deduplicate them, and sort when multiple values remain. The same producer contains `serverIdSource` with `live` and `none` values, an `isReading` lookup, and the literal `current server changed during map scan`; exact complete source-selection and named public error mapping remain deliberately unassigned.

**Current-client boundary and restrictions.** R6-035 already proves `GameEntry.Data -> CustomDataManager.Player -> DCPlayer.GetCurServerId()` and xLua exposure, but exact current-RDL member-name searches for `WorldMapState` and `CurrentServerId` return no matching managed member names. That negative name search does not prove absence of an equivalent implementation, so this checkpoint does not equate the LWBridge command `getCurrentServerId` with the R6-035 chain. SB-48 records a rejected filtered producer-dump request; it was not replayed or rerouted and supplies no evidence. Production `map_scan_status`/`map_summary` remain fail-closed because dispatcher failure/timeout mapping, `SERVER_UNAVAILABLE`/`map scan state is unavailable` conditions, complete `serverIdSource` selection and live correlation are still UNKNOWN/BLOCKED. Durable evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-shared-scan-state-producer.json`.

A positive value is retained. A zero or negative value enters `0x1400DCB29`, loads exact code `SERVER_UNAVAILABLE` (length 18) and message `current server id unavailable` (length 29), and constructs that error through `0x1404170BA` at `0x1400DCB51`. ### LWB-R6-039 — bridge package-key/runtime-material boundary (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED source identity and host response fields.** The source remains verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`, preferred image base `0x140000000`. Host function `0x14023C7EC-0x14023CB51` extracts `authorizationTicket` at `0x14023C802` (length `0x13`) and `authorizationTicketExpiresAt` at `0x14023C81F` (length `0x1C`) through helper `0x14023F10C`; the same function extracts `packageKeyEnvelope` at `0x14023C95E` (length `0x12`) and `packageKeyEnvelopeExpiresAt` at `0x14023C97B` (length `0x1B`) through that same helper. **RECOVERED runtime-material path family.** Separate host function `0x14023CC15-0x14023D5B8` constructs four sibling filenames through common path helper `0x1405C6750`: `authorization.challenge` at `0x14023D0F2`, `authorization.ticket` at `0x14023D111`, `package-key.envelope` at `0x14023D130`, and `build.manifest` at `0x14023D153`. This establishes an original runtime key-envelope artifact next to the authorization ticket/build manifest. It does **not** yet prove the direct dataflow that writes the parsed `packageKeyEnvelope` value into that file, so the contents/serialization are not inferred.

**RECOVERED proxy/package boundary.** Both embedded proxies, SHA-256 `481c636b8d0fa9bf4b8f88cba77145054b33e3701ffa42737567b211859ac400` (secure) and `c9a88ec449cc6a9929fb2f2800ebd443f9b9dcdac849e3d3e8da94281dea9794` (plain), contain `LWBRIDGE_PROFILE_RUNTIME_ROOT`, `\bridge-runtime\package-key.envelope`, `\bridge-runtime\build.manifest`, `LWKE1`, key-agreement/decrypt failures, package-decrypt/integrity/build failures and `\LastWar-xLua-Bridge\bridge-scripts.dat`. The embedded script package itself is 1,172,723 bytes, SHA-256 `a1e23419c25c76bee16942922a643381e0d38a857902569d927f22ef02c99c8d`, `LWBP` version 2, build ID `9BupJXpEgm34lybhNhbbcQ`, and contains no `LWKE1` marker. Therefore the envelope marker is separate runtime material, not part of the embedded package bytes.

**Validation, limits and impact.** Reproduce with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_bridge_runtime_material.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`; the inspector is hash-locked and read-only. `python -m py_compile tools\inspect_lwbridge_bridge_runtime_material.py` also passes. No LastWar/LWBridge process was running during this checkpoint, so there is no live correlation. The direct parsed-envelope-to-file content flow, exact proxy open/read function, key-agreement/decrypt inputs, protected package plaintext, `getWorldMapState`/`getCurrentServerId` handler implementation, propagated transport errors/readiness/null/source rules and exact `map scan state is unavailable` trigger remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. Public `map_summary`/status remain fail-closed; no production provider is enabled. Durable evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-bridge-runtime-material.json`.

### LWB-R6-040 — parsed package-key envelope persistence and invalid cleanup (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED direct parser-to-runtime-file data flow.** The source remains verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`, preferred image base `0x140000000`. In the R6-039 authorization-response parser, the successful package-key branch loads service values from `+0x1A0/+0x1A8` at `0x14023C9CE/0x14023C9D5`; these are the string data/length members of the previously recovered `package-key.envelope` runtime-path object. `0x14023C9EF` references formatter descriptor `0x140C80070`, whose exact bytes are `C0 01 0A 00`; the shared host formatter `0x1400276B0` is called at `0x14023C9FE`. Under the verified descriptor grammar this emits the parsed `packageKeyEnvelope` response string followed by one LF byte. The path pair is restored into `rcx/rdx` at `0x14023CA0B/0x14023CA0E` and passed with the formatted bytes to shared persistence helper `0x14023D8FC` at `0x14023CA11`. This closes R6-039's direct parsed-value-to-file content gap.

**RECOVERED write/finalize chain.** Persistence function `0x14023D8FC-0x14023DB02` passes its content to `0x140037A58` at `0x14023DA6B`; that dispatcher calls `0x1405BB570` at `0x140037A94`, and the latter reaches imported `ntdll.dll!NtWriteFile` at `0x1405BB5DA`. After writing the temporary material, `0x14023DAF8` calls finalizer wrapper `0x14027D760`; its `0x14027D777` call enters `0x1405B6BC0`, whose imported `kernel32.dll!MoveFileExW` call uses flag `1`, `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING`. The original therefore writes the serialized envelope through temporary runtime material and replaces the destination rather than directly truncating the final path in place.

**RECOVERED invalid/expired cleanup.** The invalid/expired branch loads the same runtime path at `0x14023CA49/0x14023CA50` and calls wrapper `0x1405B6540` at `0x14023CA57`. That wrapper reaches primitive `0x1405B8C00` at `0x1405B65BD`; the primitive directly imports `kernel32.dll!DeleteFileW` at `0x1405B8C17`. The branch then constructs the already recovered exact `KEY_ENVELOPE_INVALID` code. Missing, invalid or expired package-key material therefore removes the persisted envelope before returning that error.

**Validation, limits and impact.** Reproduce with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_runtime_material_persistence.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`; `python -m py_compile tools\inspect_lwbridge_runtime_material_persistence.py` passes and the hash-locked inspector writes `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-runtime-material-persistence.json`. This is **RECOVERED static** host behavior, not **LIVE-PROVEN**. The exact embedded-proxy open/read/parser function, key-envelope agreement/decrypt inputs, protected `bridge-scripts.dat` plaintext, plaintext `getWorldMapState`/`getCurrentServerId` handlers, propagated readiness/transport errors, exact `map scan state is unavailable` trigger and live current-client correlation remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. Public `map_summary` and status remain fail-closed; R6-040 unblocks only the host persistence seam, not an authoritative production provider.

### LWB-R6-041 — device-key/package-envelope prerequisite boundary (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED host auth/device-key boundary.** The source remains verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. Raw file offsets `0xC7F253-0xC7F853` form one `src\services\auth.rs` marker cluster containing the `HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Cryptography/vMachineGuid` fingerprint path at `0xC7F3BA`, `DEVICE_KEY_CLEANUP_PENDING` at `0xC7F364`, `packageKeyEnvelope` at `0xC7F514`, `packageKeyEnvelopeExpiresAt` at `0xC7F526`, `KEY_ENVELOPE_INVALID` at `0xC7F541`, `LWKE1` at `0xC7F580`, `device-key-cleanup.pending` at `0xC7F5FB`, the four recovered runtime filenames at `0xC7F615-0xC7F654`, and `DEVICE_KEY_MISSING` / `DEVICE_KEY_MISMATCH` / `DEVICE_KEY_UNAVAILABLE` / `KEY_ENVELOPE_EXPIRED` at `0xC7F71B-0xC7F756`. The same verified host imports `NCryptOpenStorageProvider`, `NCryptCreatePersistedKey`, `NCryptFinalizeKey`, `NCryptExportKey`, `NCryptDeleteKey`, `NCryptOpenKey`, `CryptProtectData` and `CryptUnprotectData`. This establishes an auth-service device-key prerequisite boundary; import presence alone does not assign one primitive to a specific branch or prove the storage provider/key name.

**RECOVERED matching proxy loader surface.** Both hash-locked embedded proxies contain `LWKE1` at proxy raw `0x72D4C`, URL-safe Base64 alphabet instances, `device key missing` at `0x72DF0`, `key envelope agreement failed` at `0x72E60`, `device key unavailable` / `device key mismatch` at `0x72EB0/0x72EC8`, `key envelope decrypt failed` at `0x72EE0`, `package integrity invalid` / `package build mismatch` at `0x72F08/0x72F28`, and `LWBP2|` at `0x72F40`. They also retain the R6-039 UTF-16 runtime-root, package-key envelope, build-manifest and protected-package paths. Their imports include `NCryptOpenKey`, `NCryptImportKey`, `NCryptSecretAgreement`, `NCryptDeriveKey`, `NCryptExportKey`, `BCryptImportKeyPair`, `BCryptGenerateSymmetricKey`, `BCryptDecrypt`, BCrypt hash/signature primitives, plus `GetEnvironmentVariableW`, `CreateFileW`, `ReadFile` and `GetFileSizeEx`. This narrows the protected loader to a device-key/envelope/CNG-crypto/file-I/O seam but does not prove exact call ordering, parameters, key format, KDF, cipher, nonce/tag layout or handler plaintext.

**Attempts, restrictions, validation and impact.** The existing proxy Map Scan xref inspector confirmed the ASCII `LWKE1`/package-decrypt markers but cannot locate the UTF-16 runtime path. A direct host `LWKE1` code-xref scan found no direct reference, so indirect descriptor/dataflow remains possible and is not guessed. Automatic review rejected the combined startup read, a fresh proxy pointer-table scan, a proxy import-thunk/callsite disassembly and a wider proxy raw-window read; these are recorded as SB-62–65 and were not replayed or rerouted. The reproducible verifier uses Python `3.12.10` and pefile `2024.8.26`; `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_device_key_boundary.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe` produced durable evidence at `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-device-key-boundary.json`, and `python -m py_compile tools\inspect_lwbridge_device_key_boundary.py` passes. No LastWar/LWBridge process was running, so this is **RECOVERED static** evidence only. The exact proxy open/read/decrypt call graph, device-key provider/name, envelope grammar/crypto parameters, protected `getWorldMapState`/`getCurrentServerId` handlers, readiness/transport error mapping, exact `map scan state is unavailable` trigger and live correlation remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. Public summary/status remain fail-closed. The next permitted method is a source-attributed host/device-key producer trace or a future supported live loader correlation; no Daybreak request is created from SB-62–65 alone.

### LWB-R6-042 — proxy device-key diagnostic marker vocabulary (2026-09-09; interpretation superseded by R6-043)

**RECOVERED marker vocabulary.** The source remains verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. In both embedded proxies, the exact ASCII literals `DEVICE_KEY_PROVIDER`, `DEVICE_KEY_EXPORT`, `DEVICE_KEY_FORMAT` and `DEVICE_KEY_MISSING` occur once at proxy raw offsets `0x730E0`, `0x730F8`, `0x73110` and `0x73128`. The parent-host raw offsets are `0x9FA454/0x9FA46C/0x9FA484/0x9FA49C` for secure and `0xA8FC54/0xA8FC6C/0xA8FC84/0xA8FC9C` for plain. Exact whole-host searches show the provider/export/format labels occur only inside those two embedded proxy images and have no UTF-16 occurrence. `DEVICE_KEY_MISSING` also occurs in host auth/device-key state text. R6-042 established literal identity/ownership only; R6-043 later recovered their secure-proxy branch semantics.

**Historical interpretation and supersession.** At R6-042 time these first three literals were conservatively treated as possible configuration names because only exact-string ownership had been recovered. R6-043 supersedes that interpretation with bounded secure-proxy control flow: `DEVICE_KEY_PROVIDER` is a provider-open diagnostic, `DEVICE_KEY_EXPORT` an export diagnostic, and `DEVICE_KEY_FORMAT` a public-key-structure diagnostic. R6-042's literal offsets and host/proxy ownership remain valid historical evidence.

**Reproduction, restrictions and impact.** Reproduce the original R6-042 marker finding with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_proxy_device_key_config.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe` or its documented `--output` form. Python compilation and JSON validation pass. A bounded host-function disassembly, a broader crypto-string filter and a proxy PE-metadata probe were rejected by automatic review and were not replayed or rerouted; the restriction outcomes are recorded as SB-67–69. No live LastWar/LWBridge target was available. R6-043 supplies the later branch-level interpretation and crypto contract; public summary/status remain fail-closed.

### LWB-R6-043 — secure-proxy device-key identity, public-key format and crypto contract (2026-09-09)

**Scope and source identity.** This finding corrects the interpretation of R6-042 and recovers a bounded static contract from the verified LWBridge reference `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. The analyzed embedded `xlua-proxy-secure.dll` begins at host raw offset `0x987374`, has SHA-256 `481c636b8d0fa9bf4b8f88cba77145054b33e3701ffa42737567b211859ac400`, preferred image base `0x180000000`, and is checked by the hash-locked read-only verifier `tools/inspect_lwbridge_proxy_device_key_crypto.py`. Durable output is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-proxy-device-key-crypto.json`.

**RECOVERED exact locators and correction.** The secure-proxy persisted-key helper is RVA `0x432C0-0x43380`. At `0x432E7 -> 0x432F3` it loads UTF-16 literal `Microsoft Software Key Storage Provider` (raw `0x72E10`, RVA `0x73A10`) and calls the local `NCryptOpenStorageProvider` thunk; its failure branch at `0x432FC` loads ASCII diagnostic label `DEVICE_KEY_PROVIDER`. At `0x4332D -> 0x4333C` it loads persisted key name `{2D337A4D-7E6C-49EF-9486-54F0A00D8A41}` (raw `0x73090`, RVA `0x73C90`) and calls `NCryptOpenKey`; the key-open failure branch loads `DEVICE_KEY_MISSING` at `0x43345`. Therefore R6-042's first three `DEVICE_KEY_*` literals are not runtime configuration-variable names: they are diagnostics attached to concrete branches. The previously recovered offsets remain valid; only their interpretation is superseded.

**RECOVERED device public-key export/format.** Helper RVA `0x430C0-0x432BE` calls `NCryptExportKey` twice with UTF-16 blob type `ECCPUBLICBLOB` (raw `0x708A0`, RVA `0x714A0`) to size then fill the buffer. It requires blob magic `0x314B4345` (`ECK1`) at RVA `0x431D8`, `cbKey = 0x20` at `0x431E0`, and total blob size `0x48` bytes at `0x431E6`. On success it allocates `0x41` bytes at `0x431F5`, writes prefix byte `0x04` at `0x43205`, then copies the 64 coordinate bytes from the CNG blob. `DEVICE_KEY_EXPORT` is loaded on export failure at `0x431CF/0x4328E`; `DEVICE_KEY_FORMAT` is loaded on structural mismatch at `0x43230`. The recovered proxy public representation is therefore exactly 65 bytes: `0x04 || X[32] || Y[32]`.

**RECOVERED agreement/KDF.** Helper RVA `0x3DD00-0x3E01E` accepts only the same 65-byte input (`0x41` at `0x3DD3D`) with leading `0x04` (`0x3DD47`), opens the persisted device key through RVA `0x432C0`, reconstructs an `ECK1`/32-byte `ECCPUBLICBLOB` (`0x3DDDD/0x3DDE3`), opens the same Microsoft provider, imports the peer key with `NCryptImportKey`, and calls `NCryptSecretAgreement`. Two `NCryptDeriveKey` calls at `0x3DEA4` and `0x3DEE6` use UTF-16 KDF name `TRUNCATE` (raw `0x72E80`, RVA `0x73A80`) and both require exactly `0x20` bytes (`0x3DEAD`, `0x3DEEF`). This recovers the exact CNG KDF name and 32-byte derived-key size; it does not identify the enclosing envelope field layout or caller ownership.

**RECOVERED AES-GCM primitive contract.** Symmetric-decrypt helper RVA `0x3CB60-0x3CFEA` requires a 32-byte key (`0x3CB93`), 12-byte nonce (`0x3CBA4`) and 16-byte authentication tag (`0x3CBB5`). It opens algorithm `AES` at `0x3CBF9 -> 0x3CC05`, sets property `ChainingMode` to `ChainingModeGCM` at `0x3CC1C/0x3CC23 -> 0x3CC2F`, creates the symmetric key at `0x3CD18`, and calls `BCryptDecrypt` at `0x3CDFB`. These are exact static parameters of this helper. Without its permitted caller linkage, this finding does not claim which protected object supplies each argument.

**Reproduction, validation and limits.** The verifier uses Python `3.12.10`, pefile `2024.8.26` and Capstone `5.0.6`. Reproduce with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_proxy_device_key_crypto.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe` or the `--output evidence\lwbridge-implementation\2026-09-09-r6-proxy-device-key-crypto.json` form, followed by `python -m py_compile tools\inspect_lwbridge_proxy_device_key_crypto.py` and JSON validation. The verifier passes against the exact hashes above. This is static recovery from the secure proxy only; instruction-identical behavior in the plain proxy is not claimed. A fixed raw-byte decoded-string read, bounded host provisioning-function disassembly, secure/plain function-range hash comparison and a follow-up caller-xref pass were automatically rejected and were not replayed or rerouted; SB-71–74 preserve those outcomes. Earlier successful host-wide exact-string/import-call inventory showed host copies of the same provider/GUID/ECCPUBLICBLOB vocabulary and host `NCryptCreatePersistedKey`/`NCryptFinalizeKey` callsites, but direct instruction-level provider/GUID-to-create linkage remains **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED** because the bounded host function read was rejected.

**Implementation impact and next boundary.** PM7-B no longer needs to guess the device-key provider, persisted key name, public export format, KDF name/output size or AES-GCM key/nonce/tag sizes for the secure proxy. No production summary/status provider is enabled: the exact `package-key.envelope` open/read caller, envelope grammar/field ownership, caller link into the recovered agreement/decrypt helpers, protected `getWorldMapState`/`getCurrentServerId` handlers and authoritative readiness/error propagation remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. The next regular-AI method is a permitted file-loader/caller trace from the already recovered `package-key.envelope` path and `CreateFileW`/`ReadFile` functions; host provisioning remains under investigation separately. No Daybreak request is created.

### LWB-R6-044 — shared scan-state server/source fallback and active-read mismatch behavior (2026-09-09)

**Scope and source identity.** The source remains verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. The hash-locked read-only verifier `tools/inspect_lwbridge_scan_state_source_selection.py` checks only the already recovered shared producer at preferred-image VA `0x1400F7F47-0x1400F9333`; it does not perform a fresh whole-image xref search or inspect protected bridge-script plaintext. Durable output is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-scan-state-source-selection.json`.

**RECOVERED server ownership and `isInWorld` split.** The producer extracts the `getCurrentServerId` response field `serverId` at `0x1400F81BE-0x1400F81D8` and retains that signed value in `r13`. It separately extracts world-state `homeServerId` at `0x1400F835F-0x1400F8379` into `r12`. The `isInWorld` lookup at `0x1400F8225-0x1400F8260` accepts only the boolean-tagged value; `sete` records an explicit-false marker, while missing/non-boolean input writes zero to that marker at `0x1400F8328`. The branch at `0x1400F87EE-0x1400F87F6` therefore distinguishes exact boolean false from the other input cases before choosing its server fallback path.

**RECOVERED exact-false/home-server fallback.** On the exact `isInWorld=false` path, the producer extracts the current state object's `serverId` at `0x1400F87FC-0x1400F880C`. It proceeds only when `homeServerId > 0` (`0x1400F8811-0x1400F8814`) and the existing state `serverId <= 0` (`0x1400F881A-0x1400F881D`). It then writes that positive home server as `serverId` at `0x1400F8851` and inserts `serverIdSource="live"` through the string/value helpers at `0x1400F8855-0x1400F88C4`. A nonpositive home server or already-positive state server bypasses this fallback instead of being overwritten.

**RECOVERED current-server/live branch and reading mismatch.** The alternate branch tests the recovered current server in `r13` at `0x1400F8978`. When positive, it reads the existing state `serverId` and `isReading`. The exact boolean-true `isReading` path at `0x1400F89E6-0x1400F89EB` checks whether the existing state server is positive and differs from the current server (`0x1400F89F1-0x1400F89FD`). That conjunction enters the exact `current server changed during map scan` error construction at `0x1400F8A18-0x1400F8A2F`; otherwise the producer writes the current server as `serverId` at `0x1400F8A76` and `serverIdSource="live"` at `0x1400F8ACE-0x1400F8B3C`. This closes the earlier R6-036 uncertainty around the active-read server-change guard without renaming any dispatcher failure.

**RECOVERED no-current-server/`none` fallback.** When current server is nonpositive, exact boolean true `isReading` reaches the common completion path without replacing the state (`0x1400F8C53-0x1400F8C5C`). If the state `serverId` is already positive, it is likewise preserved (`0x1400F8C62-0x1400F8C7A`). Only the remaining case writes numeric `serverId=0`, `serverIdSource="none"`, `tileWidth=0` and `tileHeight=0` at `0x1400F8C83-0x1400F8D72`. These are original producer fallbacks, not rebuild defaults.

**Validation, restrictions and remaining boundary.** Reproduce with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_scan_state_source_selection.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe` or the documented `--output` form; the verifier uses Python `3.12.10`, pefile `2024.8.26` and Capstone `5.0.6`. The existing full-function reproductions `--dump-rva 0x0F7F47` and `--dump-rva 0x151509` corroborate the producer and `map_summary` propagation boundary. `map_summary` still propagates producer failure before its `serverId` lookup and does not directly construct `map scan state is unavailable`; that separate emitter/condition remains **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**, as do protected handler/current-client linkage and exact dispatcher failure/timeout identities. SB-75 records two rejected bundled resume/document reads that contributed no evidence; SB-76 records a rejected exact-candidate envelope-format string scan, which was not replayed or rerouted and leaves the `package-key.envelope` grammar under investigation. Public summary/status remain fail-closed and no Daybreak request is created.
