### LWB-R6-046 — secure-proxy package-key path and bounded text-reader boundary (2026-09-09)

**Scope and source identity.** The source remains verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. The analyzed embedded `xlua-proxy-secure.dll` has SHA-256 `481c636b8d0fa9bf4b8f88cba77145054b33e3701ffa42737567b211859ac400` and preferred image base `0x180000000`. Read-only hash-locked verifier `tools/inspect_lwbridge_proxy_package_key_reader.py` writes durable output to `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-proxy-package-key-reader.json`.

**RECOVERED exact path construction.** Exact UTF-16 suffix `\bridge-runtime\package-key.envelope` is 36 code units / 72 bytes at proxy raw `0x70B70`, RVA `0x71770`. Unwind function `RVA 0x1B300-0x1B4BF` calls helper `0x16360` at `0x1B337`, then appends this exact suffix through direct references at `0x1B373` and `0x1B38F`; its `0x24` code-unit and `0x48` byte constants match the verified literal length.

**RECOVERED bounded read.** Enclosing loader unwind function `RVA 0x1C0A0-0x1D46F` calls the package-key path constructor at `0x1C447`, sets `R8D=0x1000` at `0x1C44D`, moves the returned path to `RDX` at `0x1C453`, and calls reader `RVA 0x1BD80-0x1C09F` at `0x1C45B`. The reader copies the caller maximum from `R8` to `RSI` at `0x1BDAC`, computes a signed length, rejects `<= 0` or `> maximum` at `0x1BEAA-0x1BEB6`, allocates the accepted length, fills the destination through internal stream helpers, and returns empty on stream failure. Its success path repeatedly removes trailing `0x0D`/`0x0A` bytes at `0x1BFAA/0x1BFBC` and NUL-terminates the shortened string at `0x1BFD5`. Internal C++ stream helper names are intentionally not inferred.

**RECOVERED opaque consumer boundary; parser still blocked.** The same enclosing loader calls the path constructor again at `0x1C8B8`, moves the returned path directly to `RCX` at `0x1C8DF`, calls unwind function `RVA 0x3F8E0-0x40A6D` at `0x1C8E2`, and tests `AL` at `0x1C8E7`. This proves that `0x3F8E0` is an exact `package-key.envelope` path consumer. It does not establish its internal grammar, parsed fields or agreement-helper ownership. SB-79 previously rejected an exact-target query inside this function; R6-046 does not inspect, replay or reroute that body. SB-81 records a separate rejected bundled repository/memory read from this resume and contributes no technical evidence.

**Validation and impact.** Reproduce with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_proxy_package_key_reader.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe` or its documented `--output` form. The verifier uses Python `3.12.10`, pefile `2024.8.26` and Capstone `5.0.6`. R6-046 and its adjacent R6-045 verifier pass, both Python inspectors compile, and all 35 `2026-09-09-r6-*.json` evidence files parse. SB-82/SB-83 record two rejected combined validation commands; those bundles were not replayed and contribute no evidence. R6-046 closes the exact proxy path/read boundary and gives a concrete opaque consumer callsite without claiming envelope field meanings. The peer-public-key field into agreement helper `RVA 0x3DD00`, envelope nonce/tag/ciphertext ownership, package-file caller/AES callsite ownership, plain-proxy parity, protected handlers and live readiness/error mapping remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. Public summary/status remain fail-closed; no Daybreak request is created.

### LWB-R6-038 — PM7-A source-aware option/count integration (2026-09-09)

**Scope and source identity.** This is an implementation checkpoint over already recovered contracts, not a new native-behavior claim. The reference `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe` was reverified at SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. R6-024 provides the only recovered data-source shapes: published `map_records` scoped by server or staging `scan_records` scoped by server plus exact run. R6-030 provides the selector: staging is used only for `isReading=true`, matching requested/scan-state server and raw nonempty `scanRunId`, including whitespace because no trimming is evidenced. R6-031 provides NULL/empty alliance handling and scan-progress row selection. R6-014 supplies the Unix-ms reward cutoff. Exact source locators and reproduction remain in those findings and their evidence JSON files.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapDataStore.SelectOptionSource` now produces one validated `MapOptionSourceSelection`, and `ReadOptionAggregatesAt` carries that context through alliance/no-alliance, resource/monster names, dispatch levels, treasure types, truck/railway reward items, all eight counts and scan-progress record selection. Runtime values are bound parameters; SQL structure is chosen only between the two recovered internal source/scope forms. Active scope reads the exact run from `scan_records` and its exact `scan_runs.id`; inactive/mismatched/missing-run scope reads published `map_records` and newest non-discarded server progress. The former persisted-only aggregate helper has been replaced rather than duplicated.

**IMPLEMENTATION POLICY and validation.** One deferred SQLite read transaction covers every aggregate in a call, preventing staged options from being paired with published counts or count/option families from observing different database generations. Deterministic fixtures deliberately conflict published versus staged records, another run on the same server, and another server; active, idle, server-mismatch, null, empty and whitespace `scanRunId` selector cases are retained. A file-backed two-connection WAL test commits a second city after the first aggregate read and proves alliances and counts remain on the original snapshot until the next call. The Release build succeeds with zero warnings/errors and the deterministic backend suite returns `ok=true` with all five groups true. Durable validation metadata is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-map-option-source-integration.json`.

**Public gate and limits (historical, superseded for completed/persisted options).** At R6-038, `LWBridgeBackend` was intentionally unchanged and `map_data_options` still returned `MAP_INDEX_UNAVAILABLE`; exact public `scanProgress` serialization/readiness remained blocked. `LWB-R7-077` now supersedes that completed/persisted public-options gap: on current-v19, one automatic Fast all-eight scan completed 2,500/2,500 and the public command exactly matched independent raw-row derivation for all counts, City alliance/no-alliance options, Resource/Monster/Zombie Boss names, Dispatch/Monster levels, Treasure descriptors, Truck/Railway retained-item options and completed scan progress, then reproduced the same aggregate metrics after database reopen. Positive active-staging live observation remains unclaimed because the current Fast all-eight source emits its logical-block set as one large batch before publication; R6-024/R6-030/R6-038 remain the recovered/deterministic authority for that selector. SB-52/53 remain historical rejected combined reads and contributed no technical finding.

### LWB-R6-026 — alternate-sort parser and ordered frontend sort state (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED source identity and reproduction.** The native source remains the verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`; the immutable recovered `MapDataPanel-C1HVeNHr.js` is SHA-256 `fc39de8154c54ee0937b6f9f9377d671e0dbad533ed2d9eda29a99d35e380f5e`. `tools/inspect_lwbridge_map_sorts.py` verifies those hashes and only fixed, bounded bytes/strings inside the already identified native `map_search` function at preferred-image VA `0x140271864-0x140276D7B` plus the recovered frontend column/toggle functions. Reproduce with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_sorts.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe evidence\lwbridge-0.3.1\frontend --json`. Durable output is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-map-sort-inventory.json`.

**RECOVERED public frontend contract.** Every kind defaults to one `updatedAt desc` sort. Selecting an absent sortable column prepends `{sortBy:key,sortOrder:'desc'}` while preserving all existing sorts; selecting the current `desc` key changes it to `asc` and moves it first; selecting it again removes it. The visible public columns are city `level/health/shield/updatedAt`; resource `level/updatedAt`; monster `level/distance/updatedAt`; truck `quality/power/itemCount/remainingLootCount/arriveTime/updatedAt`; railway `quality/power/itemCount/protectTime/updatedAt`; dispatch and ghost `level/quality/completionTime/updatedAt`; treasure `updatedAt`. `itemCount` is exposed only while an item key is selected. This proves that alternate public sorting is an **ordered multi-sort** contract, not a single replacement key.

**RECOVERED native subset.** Fixed instruction bytes pin scalar inline parser comparisons for `distance` (`0x140274B68`), `itemCount` (`0x140274BE5`), `updatedAt` (`0x140274C06`), `quality` (`0x140274C2C`), `shield` (`0x140274CDF`), `health` (`0x140274CFA`), `level` (`0x140274D6A`), `power` (`0x140274D82`), `arriveTime` (`0x140274DC2`), `protectTime` (`0x140274E0A`) and `completionTime` (`0x140274E58`). Direct expression references are pinned for `completionTime` at `0x140274ED6`, `arriveTime` at `0x140275039`, `protectTime` at `0x140275081`, and the item-count descriptor at `0x140275191`. The adjacent exact native expression inventory includes `updated_at AS sort_value_0` at raw `0x00C8A533`; `distance` and `power` literals at `0x00C8A918`/`0x00C8A920`; the item-count `currentGoods` SUM whose one-byte `0x56` descriptor is at `0x00C8A925` and SQL text starts `0x00C8A926`; `completionTime`, `remainingLootCount`, `protectTime`, `arriveTs`, truck special-UR quality, dispatch/ghost special quality and `health` expressions at raw `0x00C8A9E0-0x00C8ABB2`; and dynamic `sort_value_`, `DESC`, plus `IS NULL) ASC` page-order fragments at `0x00C8ABED-0x00C8AC48`.

**UNKNOWN/BLOCKED and implementation impact.** A fixed 18-byte SIMD comparison is present at `0x140274B1D` in the parser region, but this checkpoint does not label it `remainingLootCount` because the compared constant's producer has not been independently pinned. The shield prebuilt CASE value, distance computed value source, railway quality branch, complete per-kind native gates, and complete ordered multi-sort/null-order/tie-break assembly are also unresolved. Because the frontend normally leaves `updatedAt` behind a newly selected sort, enabling a single alternate sort in the rebuild would invent native composition semantics. Production therefore remains unchanged and non-`updatedAt` forms continue to fail closed with `MAP_QUERY_UNRECOVERED`.

**Validation and restrictions.** The verifier caught and corrected the initial handoff offset for item-count text (`0x00C8A926`, not the descriptor byte at `0x00C8A925`). The hash-locked verifier and JSON parsing pass, `git diff --check` is clean apart from line-ending warnings, the Release desktop build succeeds with zero warnings/errors, and the deterministic backend suite returns `ok=true` with profile routing, persistence, request lifetime, map persistence and map contract all true. Production source and tests are unchanged by this research checkpoint. No game or launcher process was running, so this is **RECOVERED static** evidence only and is not **LIVE-PROVEN**. SB-20 through SB-23 record rejected broad/combined inspection and bounded pointer-calculation operations; none produced evidence and none was retried or rerouted. Narrow immutable frontend reads, fixed raw-byte checks and direct string verification provided the accepted R6-026 evidence. The unresolved sort questions remain regular-AI work with concrete permitted methods; no Daybreak request is created from these restrictions alone. Validation metadata is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-map-sort-validation.json`.

### LWB-R6-027 — native sort direction branch and distance override (2026-09-09)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The source remains the verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`, plus the immutable Map Data frontend SHA-256 `fc39de8154c54ee0937b6f9f9377d671e0dbad533ed2d9eda29a99d35e380f5e`. `tools/inspect_lwbridge_map_sort_direction.py` verifies fixed bytes at preferred-image VA `0x14027525C`, `0x14027526A`, `0x140275296` and `0x1402752AF`, the `.rdata` mapping, raw `ASC` at `0x00C8A530`, raw `DESC` at `0x00C8ABFA`, and the frontend desc/asc toggle. Durable output is `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-09-r6-map-sort-direction.json`.

**RECOVERED result.** The public `asc` path selects a three-byte `ASC` direction. The public ordinary `desc` path selects four-byte `DESC`. After the descending direction has been selected, an exact inline comparison against `distance` replaces it with the three-byte `ASC` direction. This is a recovered physical SQL-direction rule; this finding does not invent why the original special-cases distance or whether a separate distance value transformation explains the visible ordering.

**UNKNOWN/BLOCKED and implementation impact.** The distance computed expression/value source, the mechanism/rationale behind physical `ASC` for the distance-desc path, and complete ordered multi-sort/null-order/tie-break assembly remain unresolved. R6-027 therefore enables no production sort; non-`updatedAt` forms still fail with `MAP_QUERY_UNRECOVERED`. A subsequent fresh quality-branch raw-range request was rejected by automatic review as SB-24 and was not retried or rerouted. The fixed-byte direction verifier uses only evidence already obtained through permitted reads. This is **RECOVERED static**, not **LIVE-PROVEN**.

### LWB-R6-047 — native map-record upsert boundary and record layout (2026-09-10)

**RECOVERED source identity and selector.** The source is the verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`, preferred image base `0x140000000`. A successful hash-locked read-only `tools/inspect_lwbridge_map_scan.py` xref pass finds the sole `INSERT INTO scan_records` string at raw `0xC8B632` / RVA `0xC8C232` and its code reference at `0x14027A542`, inside helper `0x14027A1E3-0x14027AA73`. The same helper references a published `INSERT INTO map_records` statement at `0x14027A242`. At `0x14027A20E-0x14027A22F` it first requires table-name length `12` and compares the bytes against exact ASCII `scan_records`; equality branches to the staging statement at `0x14027A535`, while the other observed path uses the published statement. The successful execution context reaches exact diagnostic label `upsert map record` at `0x14027A993`.

**RECOVERED shared record field boundary.** The helper binds one already-built record into the SQL columns. Published `map_records(kind,server_id,record_key,point_index,uuid,name,alliance_name,level,quality,power,distance,shield_end_time,updated_at,data_json)` maps, in bind order, to record offsets `+0x60`, `+0x110`, `+0x78`, `+0x00`, optional fields rooted at `+0xA8`, `+0xC0`, `+0xD8`, then `+0x10`, `+0x20`, `+0x30`, `+0x40`, `+0x50`, `+0x118`, and `+0x90`. The staging branch prepends its separate `run_id` parameter and then binds those same 14 record fields in the same order. Therefore `record_key` is already present at input-record offset `+0x78` before this helper executes; this helper is the persistence boundary, not the native identity generator.

**Reproduction, validation and limits.** Reproduce the accepted xref pass with `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_scan.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe --target "INSERT INTO scan_records" --target "INSERT INTO map_records" --target "record_key"`. Focused durable output is [`2026-09-10-r6-map-record-upsert-boundary.txt`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-r6-map-record-upsert-boundary.txt) with machine-readable summary [`2026-09-10-r6-map-record-upsert-boundary.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-r6-map-record-upsert-boundary.json). A later direct `--dump-rva` follow-up was rejected by the environment's automatic safety review and was not rerouted; the accepted finding uses only the earlier successful saved xref dump. At this checkpoint upstream callers, per-kind construction of offset `+0x78`, typed normalization/removal and scan scheduling/completion were **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**; R6-049 subsequently recovers the callers and normalized key construction. No production ingestion, live scan, or user-visible result was enabled by R6-047.

**Implementation impact / next method.** DB-03 now has a precise producer target: trace one native kind backward from the call that supplies this helper's record pointer and identify the source writes that populate `+0x78`, then correlate that kind's current-client source record and normalization before enabling any ingestion. Keep the rebuild's `MapDataStore` requirement for an already-derived `recordKey`; do not derive it from the frontend fallback chain.

### LWB-R6-048 — current-client world-point identity surface for resource data (2026-09-10)

**RECOVERED current-client source identity.** The source is the installed build `1.0.361 / 1078` `Assembly-CSharp.rdl`, size `15,640,576`, SHA-256 `871efe06819fbac438413eb96b7df8193d0be56094f3a44d5ff141e6219adcbd`, with RGMD root at file offset `0x9a88a8`, `5,828` TypeDefs and `54,474` MethodDefs. The repository-local read-only `tools/inspect_lastwar_rdl_metadata.py` reproduces the finding without loading or modifying the game.

**RECOVERED typed world-point schema and lookup axes.** `Protobuf.WorldPointInfo` is type row `4309`. Its protobuf constants identify `Id` field `1`, `ResourceInfo` field `6`, `Uuid` field `100`, `ServerId` field `102`, `SrcServerId` field `103` and `WorldId` field `104`; matching getter rows are `45249`, `45259`, `45298`, `45302`, `45304` and `45306`. `ResPointInfo` is type row `1753` and extends `PointInfo`, adding `id:int32`, `state:int32`, `gatherMarchUuid:int64`, `gatherUid:string`, `gatherAllianceId:string` and `specialType:Protobuf.SpecialType`; its world-point constructor is row `13461` and resource getters include `GetResType` row `13464` and `GetResLevel` row `13465`.

Three bounded current-client methods establish separate identity dimensions without assigning LWBridge behavior. `WorldPointManager::GetWorldTileInfo(int32 pointIndex)` is row `13619`, RVA `0x1555a0`; its CIL uses the supplied `pointIndex` as a dictionary lookup key and returns the value or null. `WorldPointManager::GetPointInfoByUuid(int64 uuid)` is row `13627`, RVA `0x1556e4`; its CIL uses the supplied `uuid` as a dictionary lookup key, obtains a tile value, then returns that tile's point object or null. `WorldPointManager::GetResourcePointInfoByIndex(int32 pointIndex)` is row `13638`, RVA `0x1558dc`; it passes `pointIndex` into an internal point lookup, compares one modified field token with literal `7`, and casts the surviving object to `ResPointInfo`. Because this RG container preserves modified CIL tokens that the current parser cannot resolve in these branches, the backing dictionary tokens and that compared field are deliberately left unnamed rather than inferred.

**Validation, restrictions and implementation impact.** Reproduce with the five exact commands recorded in [`2026-09-10-r6-current-world-point-identities.txt`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-r6-current-world-point-identities.txt); the machine-readable summary is [`2026-09-10-r6-current-world-point-identities.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-r6-current-world-point-identities.json). A combined `--references-to-type WorldPointManager`/wrapper query and a later broader `WorldPointManager` field-inventory query were rejected by automatic safety review and were not replayed or rerouted. R6-048 proves that the current client has independently typed point-index and UUID lookup inputs around resource/world-point data. At this checkpoint it did **not** prove which value becomes LWBridge's authoritative `record_key`; R6-049 subsequently recovers the original builder formula and shows ordinary indexed records use the original point integer as signed-decimal identity. The remaining current-client question is the direct capture-to-builder mapping and typed normalization, so R6-048 by itself still enables no production ingestion or live scan.

### LWB-R6-049 — original normalized record-key derivation (2026-09-10)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The source remains verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`, preferred image base `0x140000000`. The normalized-record builder is runtime function `RVA 0x2785BA-0x2793BE`; its point-name descriptor table is `RVA 0xC8BC70`; record-key selection is `0x278C47-0x278CEF`; the selected String is copied to normalized record `+0x78/+0x88` at `0x279214-0x27922B`. Signed integer formatter `RVA 0x285410-0x285556` calls decimal digit core `0x394C0-0x3968E`.

**RECOVERED result.** The builder searches four integer aliases in order: `pointIndex`, `mainIndex`, `pointId`, `index`. It preserves the original extracted integer for identity; the coordinate fallback separately decrements a copy before tile-width division. Exact `truck` or `railway`, any other record with nonempty `marchUuid`, or any record without a point index selects nonempty `uuid` when available and otherwise `marchUuid`. Every other indexed record formats the original extracted point integer as signed decimal text. The integer formatter emits `-` for negatives and its digit core uses the recovered `00..99` table with `/10000` and `/100` reduction. The resulting String is written to normalized record offset `+0x78` with its length at `+0x88`, closing the original builder-to-R6-047 persistence identity formula.

Direct calls to R6-047's shared upsert helper are exactly `0x25767E` in `0x2574CD-0x2577E2`, `0x25F368` in `0x25F01D-0x25F5C8`, and `0x270863` in `0x26EC06-0x271056`; the middle call selects exact `scan_records`, while the other two use published `map_records`. Both staging and published paths therefore consume the same normalized record layout and recovered identity slot.

**Reproduction / durable evidence.** Run `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_record_identity.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe` or add `--json`. The hash-locked verifier checks the runtime functions, exact identity branches, four-entry Rust string table, decimal formatter and direct upsert caller set. Machine-readable output is [`2026-09-10-r6-map-record-identity.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-r6-map-record-identity.json); accepted full function dumps are [`2026-09-10-r6-map-record-identity-builder.txt`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-r6-map-record-identity-builder.txt) and [`2026-09-10-r6-map-record-key-format.txt`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-r6-map-record-key-format.txt).

**Validation / limits / implementation impact.** This is original-reference static recovery, not current-client or live proof. It removes the unknown original `record_key` formula but does not establish how current build-1078 capture payloads populate the builder's point/march/UUID inputs, remaining typed field normalization/removal, scan scheduling/ack/completion, or bridge readiness. Production ingestion remains fail-closed. One separate raw string-cluster follow-up was rejected by automatic review; it was not replayed or rerouted and contributed no finding. The next PM7-C task is current-client/native-capture field mapping into this recovered builder, beginning with the resource/world-point index path already identified by R6-048.

### LWB-R6-052 — direct-scan block-grid cardinality and scalar completion prerequisite (2026-09-13)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The source is the verified `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`, image base `0x140000000`. The Map Scan state machine is `0x1400F9333-0x1400FB8D8`. It reads `tileWidth`/`tileHeight`, rejects either nonpositive value through `MAP_SIZE_UNAVAILABLE` / `world map dimensions are unavailable`, and computes/stores `totalBlocks` at `0x1400F9E57-0x1400F9EBD`. Direct-scan completion is `0x14025F9AF-0x140260A7D`; its scalar gate is `0x14025FB76-0x14025FBED`, with `INCOMPLETE_SCAN` branches at `0x14025FD06-0x14025FD3F`.

**RECOVERED result.** For each positive map dimension the native code first computes `floor((dimension + 9) / 10)` and then halves that intermediate value with upward rounding. Therefore the exact block-grid cardinality is `columns = ceil(tileWidth / 20)`, `rows = ceil(tileHeight / 20)`, and `totalBlocks = columns * rows`. This recovers cardinality only; it does not yet recover traversal order or each block request's coordinate envelope. Initial scan-state assembly at `0x1400FA5C7-0x1400FA6E0` sets `totalBlocks` to the computed value, `readBlocks=0`, `unreadBlocks=totalBlocks`, `failedBlocks=0`, and `inflightBlocks=0`.

The direct-completion path reads `totalBlocks`, `completedBlocks`, and `failedBlocks`. It first requires `completedBlocks + failedBlocks == totalBlocks`; otherwise it returns `INCOMPLETE_SCAN` / `direct map scan is incomplete`. It then requires `failedBlocks == 0`; otherwise it reaches `INCOMPLETE_SCAN` / `direct map scan contains failed batches`. A separate non-scalar input is also tested before publication and remains intentionally unlabelled, so these scalar conditions are a **prerequisite**, not the whole publication-eligibility contract.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapScanGeometry` now mirrors only the recovered block-grid cardinality, initial counters and scalar completion prerequisite. It uses the recovered `MAP_SIZE_UNAVAILABLE` error for nonpositive dimensions and is not wired in as a claim that the current bounded city/resource adapter performs full-world scanning. Deterministic checks cover exact 20-tile boundaries, 21x41 -> 2x3 -> 6 blocks, initial counters, incomplete accounting and failed-block rejection. The Release desktop build succeeds with zero warnings/errors and the deterministic desktop suite passes all six groups with real-config preservation.

**Reproduction, limits and impact.** Run `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_scan_geometry.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe --output evidence\lwbridge-implementation\2026-09-13-r6-map-scan-geometry.json`, then `python -m py_compile tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_scan_geometry.py`. The hash-locked verifier records the bounded instruction ranges and recovered literals. This is static **RECOVERED** evidence plus **IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED** rebuild infrastructure, not LIVE-PROVEN full scanning. Exact block traversal/order/request coordinates, the additional non-scalar failure input, retry/resume transitions, acknowledgement timing, native capture drain/drop gates and final publication eligibility remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. R7 should continue from those scheduler/lifecycle seams rather than guessing them.

### LWB-R6-053 — derived scan progress and pre-completion cap (2026-09-13)

**RECOVERED source identity and locators.** The verified source remains `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. Shared scan-state normalizer `0x14033EFAE-0x14033F886`, called at `0x1400F9044` and `0x140341EDC`, derives unread/progress fields in bounded range `0x14033F377-0x14033F594`. Its floating-point rounding call reaches thunk `0x14079BAC7`, whose IAT entry `0x1407B9130` is the CRT `round` import. Recovered constants are exactly `100.0`, `1000.0`, `10.0`, and `98.0`.

**RECOVERED result.** `unreadBlocks = max(totalBlocks - completedBlocks - failedBlocks, 0)`. If `totalBlocks <= 0`, `progressPercent` is `0.0`. With positive total and exact status `completed`, progress is `100.0`. Otherwise the normalizer clamps `completedBlocks + failedBlocks` into `[0,totalBlocks]`, computes the fraction of total work, multiplies by `1000`, applies CRT `round`, divides by `10`, then caps the result at `98.0`. Thus non-completed scan state cannot display 100% even when all blocks are accounted for; the completed lifecycle state is the gate that allows 100%.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapScanProgress` mirrors only those recovered derived-field rules, including one-decimal CRT-round behavior and the 98% non-completed cap. Deterministic checks cover zero total, completed+failed accounting, unread derivation, the 98% cap, completed 100%, and a half-tenth rounding case. This helper is deliberately not wired to the bounded City/Resource adapter because that adapter still lacks authoritative full-scan counters.

**Reproduction, limits and impact.** Run `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_scan_progress.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe --output evidence\lwbridge-implementation\2026-09-13-r6-map-scan-progress.json`, then `python -m py_compile tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_scan_progress.py`. This finding recovers display/status derivation from counters, not the scheduler that produces those counters. Authoritative traversal, inflight work, scan rate, retries, capture pending/drop state, drain, durable publication and full completion eligibility remain open; 100% still must not be treated as independent proof of a trustworthy published scan.

### LWB-R6-054 — scan-event counter normalization and scan-rate derivation (2026-09-13)

**RECOVERED source identity and event context.** The verified source remains `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. Common event handling recognizes `map.scan.progress` and `map.scan.complete` around `0x14034040C-0x140340514` and reaches normalizer `0x140342926-0x140342F48` through call `0x1403408F8`. The same native clock helper `0x14023F1C0` initializes the scan start timestamp at `0x1400F9ECB-0x1400F9ED0`; another world-map wait adds `0x2710` (=10,000) clock units, while the scan-rate path divides elapsed units by `1000.0`, establishing the recovered millisecond-scale arithmetic without claiming whether the underlying clock is wall-clock or monotonic.

**RECOVERED result.** Incoming `completedBlocks` and `failedBlocks` are each clamped to `[0,totalBlocks]`; if their sum still exceeds `totalBlocks`, original LWBridge returns `INVALID_SCAN_PROGRESS` / `completed and failed map blocks exceed the scan total`. Remaining work is `total-completed-failed`. Effective `inflightBlocks` is bounded to `[0,min(concurrency,remaining)]`, `readBlocks` is set from the bounded completed count, and `unreadBlocks = remaining - inflightBlocks`. `scanRate` uses bounded completed blocks divided by `max(elapsedMilliseconds,1)/1000.0`, then applies CRT `round` at hundredths precision: `round(blocksPerSecond * 100) / 100`.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapScanSchedulerProgress` mirrors only those recovered rules. Deterministic checks cover inflight bounded by concurrency, negative completed/inflight clamping, impossible completed+failed accounting, unread/read derivation, elapsed-time flooring and two-decimal scan-rate rounding. It is deliberately not wired into the bounded City/Resource adapter because that adapter does not receive authoritative full-world scan events.

**Reproduction, limits and impact.** Run `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_scan_scheduler_progress.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe --output evidence\lwbridge-implementation\2026-09-13-r6-map-scan-scheduler-progress.json`, then `python -m py_compile tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_scan_scheduler_progress.py`. The verifier hash-locks the original binary, event callsite, counter clamps, recovered error string, clock/start timestamp linkage, rate constants and CRT `round` import. A separate percentage write inside this normalizer is intentionally excluded until its exact source operand is labelled; `LWB-R6-053` remains the authoritative recovered display-progress formula. Block traversal/order/request coordinates, tick/request generation, retry policy, capture acknowledgements/drain/drop handling and final publication eligibility remain open.

### LWB-R6-055 — completion/capture safety and Stop cleanup (2026-09-13)

**RECOVERED source identity and event context.** The verified source remains `..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. On `map.scan.complete`, the common handler reads native capture counts for `pendingPoints`, `pendingMarches`, `pendingPointRemovals`, `pendingMarchRemovals`, `pendingAcks` and `dropped` around `0x1403409AF-0x140340A50`. It sums the five pending categories into the value later exposed as `nativePendingRecords`, while `dropped` remains a separate count. The same handler branches on positive dropped count before the later publishing phase. Direct completion remains function `0x14025F9AF-0x140260A7D`, and its only production caller at `0x140341374` explicitly passes zero for the helper's extra failed-batch argument.

**RECOVERED result.** Native pending records are `pendingPoints + pendingMarches + pendingPointRemovals + pendingMarchRemovals + pendingAcks`. Positive native dropped count enters failure/cleanup before publication; the original diagnostic family includes `native world capture dropped ... records`. A stopped native capture session likewise has an explicit failure diagnostic `native world capture session stopped`. Direct publication still requires `completedBlocks + failedBlocks == totalBlocks` and `failedBlocks == 0`; the helper can reject a nonzero extra failed-batch input, but the real Map Scan production caller passes that input as zero. Therefore that extra parameter is not promoted into a live scheduler metric. Two recovered Stop/cleanup paths reset shared visible state to `isReading=false`, `phase=idle`, `inflightBlocks=0`, and `resumeAvailable=false`; when the recovered active bridge/session predicate succeeds they dispatch `stopMapScan` before publishing the cleaned state.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapScanCompletionSafety` mirrors only these recovered lifecycle primitives: native pending/drop metric derivation, direct-completion validation and the recovered stopped-state values. Deterministic checks cover the pending-record sum, dropped-record detection, complete success, incomplete coverage, failed blocks, the helper's extra failed-batch input, and stopped-state reset. It is not wired into the bounded City/Resource adapter as synthetic full-world state.

**Reproduction, limits and impact.** Run `python tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_scan_completion_safety.py ..\LW\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe --output evidence\lwbridge-implementation\2026-09-13-r6-map-scan-completion-safety.json`, then `python -m py_compile tools\inspect_lwbridge_map_scan_completion_safety.py`. ### LWB-R6-057 — Clear ownership and current-live-server validation (2026-09-13)

Only after those gates pass does the handler invoke the recovered server-scoped clear operation. It then refreshes state, writes `phase=idle`, and publishes the updated state. This complements the already recovered storage rule that server-scoped clear deletes that server's scan runs/map records while preserving player marks.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapScanClearOwnership` mirrors only the recovered pre-clear ownership/server gate. ### LWB-R6-058 — transactional completion publication ownership (2026-09-13)

**RECOVERED source identity and transaction boundary.** The verified source remains `..\\LW\\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. Direct completion remains `0x14025F9AF-0x140260A7D`. After the already recovered full-coverage/zero-failed-batch gate, the publication transaction replaces the selected kind/server records from staging and then executes exact conditional transition SQL `UPDATE scan_runs SET status='completed',error=NULL,updated_at=?1 WHERE id=?2 AND status='running'` at `0x140260440`.

**RECOVERED stale-run guard and final reread.** The transition's affected-row count is tested at `0x1402604DE`; zero rows reject with exact `INVALID_SCAN` / `map scan is not running` before staging cleanup, same-server old-run pruning and commit. Only the nonzero path proceeds to `DELETE FROM scan_records WHERE run_id=?1` and `DELETE FROM scan_runs WHERE server_id=?1 AND id<>?2`. After the completion transaction, the original rereads the exact run through the recovered aggregate run reader at `0x1402608FF`; a missing run rejects with exact `INVALID_SCAN` / `map scan disappeared`. This is the recovered publication ownership/generation guard: completion cannot silently publish a run that ceased to be `running` inside the transaction.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapScanPublicationOwnership` mirrors only the two proven guards: zero affected completion-transition rows and missing post-commit run. Deterministic checks cover accepted nonzero/present cases plus the exact recovered code/message pairs. The helper is not wired into production until the common live scanner owns the same transaction and authoritative run identity.

**Storage boundary and limits.** The verified binary contains two literal `scan_blocks` SQL surfaces: schema creation and the ordered read `SELECT block_index,payload_json,status,attempts,error,updated_at FROM scan_blocks WHERE run_id=?1 ORDER BY block_index`. No literal `INSERT INTO scan_blocks`, `UPDATE scan_blocks` or `DELETE FROM scan_blocks` statement exists in this artifact. That narrows the evidence but does not prove no dynamically assembled/indirect writer exists, so per-block retry/checkpoint semantics remain **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. **RECOVERED source identity and terminal branch.** The verified source remains `..\\LW\\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. The recovered branch tests the resulting failure-list length at `0x140341226`.

Only an empty failure list enters the `publishing` phase at `0x1403412C2`, publishes that state, and invokes direct completion at `0x140341374`. This makes positive failed blocks and nonempty `lastError` explicit pre-publication blockers. Whitespace-only `lastError` is still nonempty; the recovered code does not trim it. `pendingAcks` remains part of the separately reported `nativePendingRecords` aggregate from `LWB-R6-055`, but it is not an input to this recovered terminal failure-list branch. ### LWB-R6-060 — active-event identity admission (2026-09-14)

**RECOVERED source identity and event path.** The verified source remains `..\\LW\\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. The shared event path identifies `map.scan.progress`, `map.scan.complete` and `map.scan.error`, then calls admission helper `0x140344DCF-0x140344F1D` before progress/completion/error processing. The recovered helper requires current `isReading=true`.

**RECOVERED identity semantics.** If an incoming event provides a nonempty `scanRunId`, it must byte-match the active scan run ID or the event is rejected. If an incoming event provides a positive `serverId`, it must equal the active scan-state server ID or the event is rejected. Missing/empty run identity and zero/missing server identity are tolerated by the original helper; the rebuild must not synthesize those fields merely to make the gate pass. This is the recovered stale/late-event ownership guard around scan events, but it does not establish game-side delivery ordering or prove every event carries both identity fields.

**STATIC/OFFLINE-VERIFIED.** Run `python tools\\inspect_lwbridge_map_scan_event_admission.py ..\\LW\\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe --output evidence\\lwbridge-implementation\\2026-09-14-r6-map-scan-event-admission.json`. ### LWB-R6-061 — Start world-map readiness and live-server admission (2026-09-14)

**RECOVERED Start readiness sequence.** The verified source remains `..\\LW\\lwbridge-0.3.1.exe`, SHA-256 `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. When the current world state is not ready, the original issues `enterWorldMap` with a 5,000 ms request timeout, establishes a 10,000 ms readiness deadline after successful dispatch, and polls world readiness every 500 ms. If `isInWorld` has not become true by the deadline, Start fails with exact `WORLD_MAP_FAILED` / `failed to enter world map`.

**RECOVERED live-server gate.** After world readiness, Start rereads the current server and requires a positive `serverId` plus exact `serverIdSource="live"`. Failure routes to exact `SERVER_UNAVAILABLE` / `current server id unavailable`. Only after this gate does the Start state machine continue toward type/mode/geometry validation, scan-run construction and `startMapScan`. This finding recovers readiness/admission only; it does not recover block traversal, retry or acknowledgement drain.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `MapScanStartOwnership` now mirrors the recovered time constants, enter-world predicate and fail-closed world/live-server validators. A dedicated module-initializer check in `tests/LWBridge.Desktop.Checks/MapScanStartReadinessChecks.cs` executes as part of the existing deterministic check assembly without altering its main test program. Native assertions live in `tools/lwbridge_map_scan_world_readiness_assertions.py`; `tools/package_lwbridge_map_scan_world_readiness_evidence.py` runs those assertions before writing `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-14-r6-map-scan-world-readiness.json`. Production Start still needs the common live scanner to call this recovered sequence against authoritative bridge/world state.

### LWB-R7-008 — ordinary Manual Start full Player City scan (2026-09-16)

The accepted live run reached `2,500/2,500` logical blocks with `0` failed blocks, published `2,592` Cities and reopened exactly `2,592` Cities. Scan wall time reported by the ordinary service was `51.5242146 s`; the process exited `0`, official v16 bytes were restored and no game/LWBridge process remained.

**Completeness cross-check.** A dedicated `--live-current-full-map-coverage` run immediately afterward independently covered `10,000/10,000` AOIs in `250` primary requests with zero cleanup requests, two bounded first-attempt timeouts that recovered, and published/reopened `2,254` Cities. Because sequential live scans produced different City populations, exact City count is not used as a fixed invariant. The accepted invariant is exact AOI coverage plus zero failed logical blocks and publication/reopen count equality. Production previously exposed a real loss mode when records were persisted footprint-by-footprint: only the first 100-tile Y band survived. `LWB-R7-008` removes that loss by accumulating all City records globally until the full AOI union is proven, matching the already-proven dedicated-run semantics.

**Validation and remaining boundary.** Release build is `0` warnings / `0` errors; deterministic profile/persistence/request-lifetime/map-persistence/map-contract/bridge-control checks remain green, including 250 simulated native responses -> 2,500 logical captures with Cities preserved at both map extremes. Full-City progress is currently correctness-first and coarse: the source returns all 2,500 logical captures only after the 10,000-AOI union is complete, so incremental active progress is the next fidelity task. ### LWB-R7-010 — ordinary Manual Start full Monster scan (2026-09-16)

**LIVE-PROVEN production result.** Ordinary `map_scan_start` now accepts Monster-only scans on the normal `1000x1000` world and reuses the same continuous 25x10 native AOI target grid as Player City. The cleaned-candidate acceptance required the exact union of all `10,000/10,000` LOD0 AOIs before returning `2,500` logical block captures, reached `2,500/2,500` with `0` failed blocks, transactionally published `6,934` Monster rows, reopened the database and read exactly `6,934` again. A prior live run on the pre-cleanup candidate published/reopened `7,358`, reinforcing that exact live Monster count is not a fixed invariant.

**Authoritative Monster source and scope.** The working source is `WorldScene.MarchDataManager.GetAllMarchesByCS`, not `WorldPointManager._pointInfos`. Each candidate must be classified by the game as `IsMonsterOrBoss()` and production additionally requires `IsMonster()` or `IsBoss()` before accepting it. `monsterId` is resolved through `DataCenter.MonsterTemplateManager.TryGetMonsterTemplate(monsterId)`, and production requires authoritative template `id`, `name` localization key and `level`; it preserves config `type`/`special`/`boss` plus live UUID, coordinates, HP/rally/lifetime fields when present. This deliberately covers the broad map Monster/Boss population and is **not** hard-coded to Zombie Invasion.

**Validation and boundary.** Release build is `0` warnings / `0` errors; the current-v16 package round-trip gate passes; all six deterministic groups remain green; and source-level regression covers 250 simulated native footprints -> 2,500 logical Monster captures, including opposite map extremes. Sanitized evidence is [`2026-09-16-r7-manual-full-monster-proof.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-16-r7-manual-full-monster-proof.json). Production still accepts one selected kind at a time (`city`, `resource`, or `monster`); Resource complete-run acceptance, Truck/Railway/Dispatch/Ghost/Treasure, mixed selections and all-eight remain open. Monster readable names use the separate `lastwar_localize` presentation path, now restored and offline-tested under `LWB-R7-011`.

### LWB-R7-011 - recovered offline Last War localization and Monster-name presentation (2026-09-16)

**RECOVERED reference contract.** The verified original `lwbridge-0.3.1.exe` SHA-256 is `2a2de09b35bb6a03f26b5e05f949f3aea6215f294127e605d7d78481f855cdff`. `tools/inspect_lwbridge_map_scan.py` locates the original `lastwar_localize` command near inspector-reported RVA `0x23509e`, its locale-loader helper near `0x2ba43f`, and the per-key lookup/fallback helper near `0x20b716`. The command compares the requested-key count against `0xC8`, so at most **200 keys** are accepted; larger requests fail as `TOO_MANY_LOCALE_KEYS`. For each accepted key, lookup order is the requested locale first, English second when the requested locale is not `en`, then the key string itself. The embedded manifest is version `1`, source version `23443`, format `json-gzip`, key count `1009`, and lists nine locales: `en`, `zh-CN`, `zh-TW`, `ja`, `ko`, `vi`, `id`, `ru`, `pt`.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** `LastWarLocaleService` restores that request/fallback behavior behind production `lastwar_localize`. The rebuild cache location `%LOCALAPPDATA%\LWBridgeRebuild\locales` is an explicit **IMPLEMENTATION POLICY**: the repository does not embed game-owned locale payloads. `tools/extract_lwbridge_locale_cache.py` pins the reference executable hash, recovers the embedded manifest, requires source version `23443`, verifies every declared compressed size/SHA-256, gzip-decompresses each payload, requires a JSON object with string values, and atomically writes the verified cache. Production loading independently rechecks manifest shape, basename-only locale filenames, size, SHA-256, gzip and JSON/string-value validity before serving names.

**Validation.** All nine extracted locale files verified at `1009` keys each. `LastWarLocaleChecks` covers requested-language precedence, English fallback, key echo, default English, the exact 200/201 boundary, unsupported/missing/invalid/tampered cache failures, and production `LWBridgeBackend.InvokeAsync("lastwar_localize", ...)`. The real local-cache proof resolves representative Monster keys through the production backend in both English and `zh-CN`. The recovered Map Data frontend already collects `monsterNameKey` values, calls `lastwar_localize` using the current UI language, and reruns localization when language changes; its renderer deliberately treats a returned value equal to the key as unresolved and shows the localized `Unknown Monster` fallback. A synthetic isolated Playwright fixture exercises that exact frontend path: two fake Monster rows render translated English names, switching to `zh-CN` remounts the recovered language provider, reopening Monster renders the Chinese fixture names, `lastwar_localize` call count increases from `2` to `4`, and frontend failures remain empty. No game text from the reference cache is committed in the fixture.

**Limits / implementation impact.** This closes the downstream readable-Monster-name blocker at **IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED** scope; it does not promote localization to current-client LIVE-PROVEN status and does not claim every non-Map-Data runtime-name consumer has been exercised. The cache is intentionally tied to the verified reference manifest/source version and fails closed if that evidence changes. Monster acquisition remains separately LIVE-PROVEN under `LWB-R7-010`. With presentation restored, the owner-requested next engineering target returns to full-world scan performance toward roughly one minute while preserving exact AOI coverage/publication guarantees; Resource remains the next category-acceptance target on the same shared engine. Durable machine evidence: [`2026-09-16-r7-monster-localization.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-16-r7-monster-localization.json).

### LWB-R7-012 - current-v17 full-world scan-speed planner and single-Stop UI-state repair (2026-09-16)

**LIVE-PROVEN measured footprint and performance.** The current-v17 game returns a stable `5x10` LOD0 `_curViewIndex` footprint (`50` AOIs) for the production coverage request even though the prior validator required only a `4x10` subset. Bounded measurements proved `50` cells at the first edge (`x=0..4,y=0..9`), three repeated interior reads (`x=47..51,y=40..49`) and the far edge (`x=95..99,y=90..99`), with native response-flag transition, stable target tile and pre-response camera restoration true in every sample. Production full-world planning therefore changes from `25x10=250` sequential requests to `20x10=200`, a 20% request reduction. The hard safety invariant is unchanged: the union of returned native AOIs must still equal all `10,000/10,000` cells before the source can return `2,500` logical captures. A deterministic negative regression removes ten fifth-column cells and proves `9990/10000` is rejected rather than published.

**LIVE-PROVEN Monster acceptance on the new planner.** Two ordinary Manual Start full-world Monster runs completed `2,500/2,500` logical blocks with zero failed blocks and publish/reopen equality. Normal (`concurrency=8`) published/reopened `3,850` current monsters/bosses in `43.6199383 s`; Fast (`concurrency=20`) published/reopened `11,500` in `70.7457797 s`. The live population changed substantially between runs, so row count and exact wall time are not invariants. Fast retains the recovered concurrency field but the current native camera path remains intentionally single-owner/sequential; no invented parallel navigation is claimed, so Fast is not guaranteed to beat Normal on every live run. The owner-facing target is now actually implemented at roughly one minute plus live variance: the Fast acceptance was about `1m10.7s`, while a separate Normal run was `43.6s`.

**Single-Stop and live progress integration.** The normal window previously had an integration race: `ManualMapScanCommandService` changed state internally, but no producer emitted the already-recovered `bridge://map-scan-status` event, while periodic `map_summary` could return saved-profile scan state instead of the active Manual run. That could leave progress visually stale and make the first Stop return/display `cancelling` until a second click supplied another status refresh. The service now publishes real status changes; `LWBridgeWindow` forwards them on `bridge://map-scan-status`; `map_summary` reads the active Manual status/run scope; and `map_scan_stop` waits for the owned run's terminal state before returning. A dedicated current-v17 live proof completed one real block, issued one Stop, and returned `finalPhase=idle`, `stopped=true`. Deterministic integration coverage additionally proves Start -> active `map_summary` scanning -> one Stop -> idle summary and a `cancelling` -> `idle` event sequence.

**Validation and boundary.** Release desktop/check builds are `0` warnings / `0` errors; all six deterministic groups pass; the generated frontend is unchanged and all `36` browser checks pass; current-v17 compatibility revalidates exact package/critical anchors; `git diff --check` passes; and game/launcher/app are stopped afterward. A broader historical current-block proof hit its unrelated data-dependent zero-city edge-search precondition before its Stop stage; the dedicated single-Stop proof isolates the reported defect and passes. Sanitized evidence is [`2026-09-16-r7-map-scan-speed-stop.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-16-r7-map-scan-speed-stop.json). Resource complete-run acceptance is next on the same shared engine.

### LWB-R7-001 — bounded current-game resource acquisition and visible Map Data row (2026-09-10)

**LIVE-PROVEN source identity and locator.** Fresh read-only runtime evidence identifies official app `1.0.361`, build `1078`. Launcher log lines `40124`, `40131`, `40135` and `40136` show local manifest `1078`, Lua v14 with size `41301710`/CRC `2371889527`, successful pack-index verification and official `Game/LastWar.exe` startup at `2026-09-10 03:01:45.343`. The instrumented v14 capture package has SHA-256 `028e32ca71cf318758ab696ca05628ec30a28b429646e8bd6834dc0287bfffa0` and matches that version/size/CRC. The resulting diagnostics file has SHA-256 `6447d30f373a76fbfe416a894546f8797ea71e20e44d9f06d3bfd61cd367cbce` and `capturedAt=2026-09-09T19:02:27Z`, about 42 seconds after launcher startup. It contains `15,293` accumulated world records, `8,000` `resource_point` records, `10,000` covered blocks and `65` completed batches. The exact selected row is `WorldPointManager._pointInfos` point `1006`, server/srcServer `2212`, `x=5`, `y=1`, `level=3`, `pointType=7`, `resourceType=wood`, `resourceTypeId=2`, `uuid=0`, `worldId=0`, with `gatherTimeStatus=occupied_time_unavailable`.

**IMPLEMENTATION POLICY and exact rebuild path.** `FirstLiveResultImporter.ImportOneResource` in `src/LWBridge.Desktop/FirstLiveResultImporter.cs` accepts only a hashable diagnostics file containing a usable real `resource_point`, maps that category to recovered public kind `resource`, uses the source point ID as decimal `recordKey`/`pointIndex`, preserves the captured timestamp as `updatedAt`, and writes only source-backed indexed fields to `MapDataStore`. `LWBridgeBackend` exposes the recovered `{serverId,counts,scanState}` summary shape only in this bounded first-live mode so the existing frontend can select the imported server; ordinary production summary/options remain unavailable. `LWBridgeWindow` isolates the mode in an in-memory store and dedicated WebView data directory, suppresses auto-launch, selects the Resource tab for capture, and changes only the selected row's status to `—` because the recovered frontend would otherwise infer Idle solely from absent gather identifiers.

**Validation and visible result.** The focused record was programmatically extracted from the hash-identified full live capture into [`2026-09-10-first-live-resource-capture.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-first-live-resource-capture.json), preserving the source-capture hash. Release capture exits `0`; [`2026-09-10-first-live-resource-ui.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-first-live-resource-ui.json) and [`2026-09-10-first-live-resource.png`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-first-live-resource.png) show server `2212`, Resource count `1`, `1 items`, `5,1`, `Unknown resource`, level `3`, status `—`, and `9/10/2026 3:02:27 AM`. `resourceType=wood` is not promoted to the original `resourceNameKey` because that mapping is unrecovered; `occupied_time_unavailable` is not promoted to Idle/Gathering. The standard production `map_data_options` remains `MAP_INDEX_UNAVAILABLE` in the same capture.

**Limits and next step.** This proves a real current-game source record reached the rebuilt storage/query/display path, but the rebuilt application did not own the game session or acquire the row through production `map_scan_start`; the adapter replays the captured current-game diagnostics. The guarded v14 production-scanner attempt loaded/registered but did not produce a terminal status result, so exact scan serializer/lifecycle completion remains **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED**. Computer Use initialization was rejected by the environment's automatic safety review before any UI action and was not rerouted. The next work is to connect the supported R5 bridge/session to this proven current resource source and production scan lifecycle, then repeat the same source-correlated display with a freshly acquired row.

### LWB-PM12-001 — partial-install recovery and shared helper ownership (2026-09-10)

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED scope.** PM review 12 identified that `tools/run_live_resource_probe.py` armed rollback only after all three installed files were replaced, so a replacement-2/3 failure could leave a partial candidate installed. The helper now creates and SHA-256-verifies backups for `LWScripts.data`, `LWScripts.txt` and `version.txt`, persists `runtime/recovery.json` before the first replacement, records install/restore stages, verifies each restored file against the original hash, and leaves recovery state intact if cleanup itself fails. A later helper invocation restores a pending interrupted operation before it can proceed. The helper also takes a nonblocking Windows file lease over the shared live-resource runtime so separate helper processes cannot concurrently own the installation/command/result paths. These recovery/lease choices are rebuild **IMPLEMENTATION POLICY**; they are not claimed as original LWBridge lifecycle semantics.

**Source identity and locators.** The behavior is implemented in `tools/run_live_resource_probe.py` SHA-256 `0a804cc3600e5e3864b6d5d42ec25602125cd2c9fc951ca71efb306524d8e78f`: `OperationLease` at line 57; exact triplet snapshot at line 104; backup/verification at line 380; restore at line 411; install staging at line 428; durable recovery state at lines 435–486; and owned execution beginning at line 531. The isolated regression source is [`2026-09-10-pm12-recovery-regression.py`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-pm12-recovery-regression.py), SHA-256 `c2bbac10d6e45d7454fd2206863869039fb50c94998d67787ccf181c31945465`; its saved output is [`2026-09-10-pm12-recovery-regression.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-pm12-recovery-regression.json), SHA-256 `e329112223651e770a7d53197e1eb013d81676734758557c05ef68b0ced9c16e`. The original defect remains historically documented by [`2026-09-10-pm12-partial-install-repro.py`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-pm12-partial-install-repro.py) against audited revision `749b8e3`.

**Reproduction and result.** Run `python evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-pm12-recovery-regression.py`. Disposable-file failures injected at install replacements 1/2/3 all restore the exact original bytes. Failures injected at restore replacements 1/2/3 preserve pending recovery state and a subsequent recovery restores all originals and clears the pending marker. An interrupted candidate install is recovered on the next invocation. A separate child Python process acquires the shared lease; the parent process is rejected while it is held and succeeds after release. A combined primary+cleanup failure preserves both error messages and retains recovery state. `python -m py_compile tools/run_live_resource_probe.py evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-pm12-recovery-regression.py` also passes. No installed game file or game process is used by this regression.

**Limits and implementation impact.** This closes PM12-A's isolated partial-install restoration and cross-process helper-ownership defects. It does **not** close PM12-A as a whole: the production helper still selects its default installation path internally and its historical close fallback still targets `LastWar.exe` by image name. The attempted patch that added exact PID/path validation and normal-close/scoped fallback was rejected by the current environment's automatic safety review and was not rerouted through another executor. Therefore further installed-file mutation testing remains gated until exact installation/session/PID targeting can be implemented through an allowed path. PM12-B/C isolated work can continue independently.

### LWB-PM12-002 — bounded lifecycle and immutable result ingestion (2026-09-10)

### LWB-PM13-003 — cancellation/store-commit and helper ownership (2026-09-11)

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** Review 13 identified two rebuild lifecycle races rather than a recovered original-LWBridge contract: correlated import mutated SQLite before the final cancellation/closed check, and the Python helper could be started before cleanup ownership was registered. The bounded adapter now validates into a prepared resource record with no store mutation, then uses the existing `stateGate` to make `reading -> committing` the explicit completion-versus-cancel decision. Stop/Close that wins before that transition prevents persistence; after the transition, the validated commit is allowed to finish and returns success instead of a contradictory cancelled result. This ordering is **IMPLEMENTATION POLICY**, not claimed original behavior.

Helper startup now reserves `helperStartPending` cleanup ownership before `Process.Start` and transfers it to `activeHelperProcess` immediately after start; concurrent Close cannot orphan a started child. Deterministic barriers cover Stop-before-commit, Close-before-commit, Stop-after-commit-decision, and Close after process start but before active registration. The existing duplicate-Start, running-helper Stop/Close, supervised timeout, immutable-result correlation and all three PM12 recovery/session/scoped-close regressions remain green. Release build is 0 warnings/errors, desktop checks report all six groups true with real-config preservation, frontend integrity and 35 browser checks pass, and no game/helper live acquisition was performed. ### LWB-PM13-008 — passive owner-assisted Resource Search/reopen evidence package (2026-09-11)

**IMPLEMENTATION POLICY + IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED.** The owner can supply screenshots/descriptions but must not be asked for commands, logs, hashes or database inspection. `OwnerEvidenceRecorder.cs` and `LWBridgeWindow.cs` therefore add a passive `--owner-evidence <directory>` mode for the ordinary app. It records only browser-originated normal Resource `map_search` request IDs/payloads/results that already occurred, then polls the already-rendering `.map-table--resource` using the PM13-006 coordinate-span snapshot. It never clicks Search, starts a scan, drives Windows, invokes the live helper, modifies rows or clears recovery state. The native owner-evidence mode additionally rejects scan/state-changing Map Data commands with `OWNER_EVIDENCE_READ_ONLY`, so accidental Start/Clear/action input cannot become a live operation. This collection mechanism is rebuild policy, not recovered original LWBridge behavior.

`tools/collect_owner_resource_evidence.py` and repo-root `Start Owner Resource Check.cmd` create a unique `%LOCALAPPDATA%\LWBridgeRebuild\owner-evidence\<UTC>-<exeSha8>-<dllSha8>` bundle. Preflight records exact build/git/current-client/profile/store/process/recovery state, runs the established read-only runtime inspector and live-helper `--check-only` compatibility gate, and refuses a normal owner session if LWBridge/Last War/launcher or recovery ownership is already active. Postflight records the actual same-request Search/render correlation, read-only Resource rows, official-runtime fingerprint, cleanup state and app exit. Per-session preflights are preserved independently, UI JSONL files are hashed, and only the latest Search request can satisfy session completion. Runtime package/xLua/Assembly/Lua version fingerprints must equal preflight. Zero-row Search ends as `COMPLETE_EMPTY`; a saved row enables one reopen session only, which must use the same app hashes/profile and return the same server/record/point/x/y/level/`updatedAt` signature.


**Validation.** The exact Release apphost is 163,328 bytes, SHA-256 `eb1cd35eebe28bd7853a79ec5709257ce29f0b2cc2eca71d9d44ab4f7913f4d8`; the managed `LWBridge.Desktop.dll` is 442,880 bytes, SHA-256 `7f21ad24d8852deb4ed57afed81473a99054e44f0e04a0c88f0992c2daa935ec`. Both identities are recorded because the .NET apphost hash alone does not identify managed-code changes. The build has zero warnings/errors. The real `.cmd` entry point passes collector `--self-test` and read-only `--preflight-only`; deterministic desktop checks cover Resource Search recognition, result-derived target, exact-row acceptance, stale-time rejection, empty-result/empty-render correlation, malformed nonempty rejection, read-only command blocking and durable request-id JSONL recording. Frontend integrity, PM13 feedback/proof browser checks, preference/transport checks, three PM12 isolated regressions and all 35 standard browser checks pass.

**Restriction and impact.** The beginner guide instructs only normal saved Resource Search/reopen after PM approval. Screenshots complement the automatically captured native Search/result/render chain; SQLite remains supplementary only. No fresh Resource Start was executed. SB-97 remains **UNKNOWN/BLOCKED** for the first/second fresh acquisitions, and neither the collector, shortcut nor owner is permitted to recreate that denied operation. Evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-11-pm13-owner-evidence-package.json`.

### LWB-R7-013 - Monster result usability and source-backed countdown (2026-09-17)

The rebuilt Monster result page can submit one exact level as equal positive `minLevel`/`maxLevel`; arbitrary Monster ranges remain rejected. Localized visible-name search resolves displayed names through `lastwar_localize` and sends matching `monsterNameKeys` (bounded by the recovered 200-key localization cap). Monster rows now render a local ticking countdown when a supported source timestamp is positive and meaningful; otherwise the cell remains `-`. The browser regression proves the text changes over time without issuing a new `map_search`. Deterministic backend cases prove sorted level options, exact-level inclusion, arbitrary-range rejection, localized name-key inclusion, `Zombie` raw-schema false-positive rejection and malformed `monsterNameKeys` validation. Release builds, the six-group deterministic suite, generated-frontend check, 36 browser checks and the frontend visual comparison all pass; the final fresh comparison has zero high-delta pixels and only negligible antialiasing drift in one of 32 pairs. ### LWB-R7-018 - Monster Protection enrichment must not discard base scans (2026-09-17)

**Status:** owner-observed failure DIAGNOSED; correction IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED and subsequently LIVE-PROVEN by `LWB-R7-019`. After the v18 client update, the owner reported Monster scan progress repeatedly reaching about 4.5%, resetting to 0%, then repeating. The persisted scan state proves the failure was deterministic rather than a generic v18 AOI break: a Monster-only 2,500-block run was discarded with two early blocks each exhausted after two attempts, both carrying exact error `Fast world batch failed: monster_invasion_protection_response_timeout`.

**Cause and correction.** The new Invasion/Zombie Boss protection lookup is optional row enrichment, but the first implementation promoted a missing `MonsterInvasionBossDetail` response to `fail_bulk_aoi`, so one unresolved shield-detail request invalidated otherwise healthy Monster acquisition. Production now keeps base Monster acquisition authoritative and independent: protection transport/send/response timeout leaves the affected row `monsterProtectionKnown=false`, clears timed-out pending UUID correlation, records target/request/ready metrics, and continues the AOI batch. C# accepts only consistent `ready <= request <= target` counters rather than requiring every optional response. Invasion-protection-eligible rows with unknown detail explicitly do not fall back to the separate `zMBossInfo` shield family, so scan continuity does not fabricate a countdown.

**Validation and limit.** Deterministic full-world coverage now proves an incomplete protection response still returns all 2,500 captures, retains unresolved-detail metrics, and suppresses unrelated shield fallback; known-active and known-inactive protection cases remain covered. Lua parse, six deterministic groups, Release build and `git diff --check` are clean. The failed session's deferred candidate was recovered to exact original v18 package SHA-256 `e4f875a8227ffcf907aac60ba20c60e0830236cba070c4a6de9eacba63d9d2a1`, compatibility returns `ok=true`, and no recovery journal/process remains. `LWB-R7-019` supersedes the pending-live-test statement with complete v18 runs below the owner's 90-second ceiling. Positive Zombie Boss Remaining is still not LIVE-PROVEN because those live populations contained no active positive deadline. Evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-17-r7-monster-protection-scan-resilience.json`.

### LWB-R7-019 - automatic Zombie Boss protection detail without scan-wide slowdown (2026-09-17)

**RECOVERED current-v18 request contract.** Source identity is current content v18 package SHA-256 `e4f875a8227ffcf907aac60ba20c60e0830236cba070c4a6de9eacba63d9d2a1` (41,296,021 bytes, CRC32 738537259). In `UI/UIWorldPoint/Component/WorldMonsterDes.luac`, `WorldMonsterDes.RefreshData` checks exact `template.special == WorldMonsterSpecialType.MonsterInvasionBoss`, then performs `SFSNetwork.SendMessage(MsgDefines.MonsterInvasionBossDetail, view.ctrl.serverId, data.uuid)`. The response handler routes the message to `DataCenter.MonsterProtectionManager:OnGetDetail(msg)`; UI refresh reads `GetMonsterProtectionEndTime(uuid)` and displays the deadline relative to authoritative server time. This explains the owner's observation: Jump loads the object, while clicking the Zombie Boss opens/refreshes the detail path that requests protection state. Other Monster specials do not use this branch.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED automatic batching.** The scanner now reproduces that detail action for every exact Invasion Zombie Boss while its AOI is loaded. Requests preserve the raw wire UUID, use the owned scan server as the rebuild equivalent of `view.ctrl.serverId`, deduplicate UUIDs, and bind collection to the exact profile/session/challenge/game PID/scan run/server. Base AOI capture never waits for a per-boss three-second timeout. Boss-bearing batches receive only a host-side 100 ms maximum settle, and one still-unanswered UUID retry may be sent after 40 ms; both values are explicit **IMPLEMENTATION POLICY** measured against the owner's under-90-second requirement, not recovered original constants. A single post-acquisition collector validates exact run identity/cardinality, waits once for outstanding events, and merges only authoritative received `isProtected`/`protectionEndTime` values. Detail failure is fail-soft: the complete Monster index publishes and the affected Remaining stays unknown.

**LIVE-PROVEN performance and transport.** The first corrected v18 run removed the repeated per-AOI detail waits and completed in 69.0848678 seconds, publishing/reopening 7,160 rows, but proved that final-only enumeration is invalid because 63 captured eligible bosses had unloaded before the final map position. Moving request dispatch into each loaded AOI completed in 74.5309576 seconds and produced 45 authoritative detail replies for 80 automatic requests. A rejected in-Lua dwell experiment failed closed and published nothing; it was removed. The final host-paced/one-retry implementation completed all 2,500 blocks with zero failed/unread blocks in 82.9616908 seconds Normal (`concurrency=8`), publishing and reopening exactly 10,350 dynamic Monster rows. It identified 81 eligible bosses, issued 81 requests plus 81 bounded retries, and received 50 authoritative details. The remaining 31 timed out and stayed unknown without resetting progress or discarding the scan.

**Validation, limits and impact.** Release checks build with zero warnings/errors and the deterministic suite returns `ok=true`, `failures=[]`. Full-world regressions cover received active protection populating persisted `shieldEndTime`, received inactive protection suppressing Remaining, unresolved detail preserving all 2,500 captures, no unrelated `zMBossInfo` fallback, and one final run-bound collector. The final dynamic live population returned zero active positive protection deadlines, so a visibly ticking positive Remaining is not claimed LIVE-PROVEN; `-` was correct for received inactive and unresolved rows in that run. Exact response yield is not a fixed invariant. Durable aggregate evidence and reproduction are in [`2026-09-17-r7-zombie-boss-protection-batch.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-17-r7-zombie-boss-protection-batch.json).

### LWB-R7-020 - ordinary UI accepts deduplicated protection carry-over (2026-09-17)

**Owner-observed ordinary-path failure.** After R7-019's technical full-scan proof, the owner's normal UI scan still stalled. The persisted production run was `monster`-only over 2,500 logical blocks and ended `discarded` with 31 failed blocks and zero completed blocks. Twenty-nine failures carried exact error `Fast world Monster Invasion protection detail counters are inconsistent.`; two carried the native response/restoration contract error while concurrent workers were retrying. No live identity or coordinate is retained in durable evidence.

**Root cause and correction.** R7-019 intentionally deduplicates Zombie Boss UUID requests across overlapping AOI footprints. Therefore a later footprint can validly contain one eligible target whose network request was sent in an earlier overlap and whose reply is now ready: `targetCount=1`, `newRequestCount=0`, `readyCount=1`. The ordinary C# batch parser incorrectly enforced `readyCount <= newRequestCount`, so valid carry-over responses invalidated the whole batch. Production now validates the actual per-footprint invariants independently: `newRequestCount <= targetCount` and `readyCount <= targetCount`. The run-bound final collector remains responsible for exact deduplicated target cardinality and authoritative response merging.

**Validation and owner gate.** Deterministic full-world coverage now includes the exact `1/0/1` carry-over case and still returns all 2,500 captures. The complete six-group deterministic suite is `ok=true`, Release build has zero warnings/errors, and `git diff --check` is clean. The failed owner session left a temporary candidate installed; `recover_overview_pending_current.py` restored exact original v18 package SHA-256 `e4f875a8227ffcf907aac60ba20c60e0830236cba070c4a6de9eacba63d9d2a1`, compatibility is `ok=true`, and the recovery journal is gone. No post-fix live scan is claimed here. Owner acceptance is now: first prove one ordinary Normal Monster scan reaches 100% without reset/stall, then inspect Zombie Boss Remaining. Evidence: [`2026-09-17-r7-owner-ui-dedup-counter-fix.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-17-r7-owner-ui-dedup-counter-fix.json).

### LWB-R7-024 - serialize MonsterInvasionBossDetail and decouple it from scan progress (2026-09-18)

**RECOVERED current-v18 correlation contract.** Exact official v18 `Net/Config/MsgMap.luac` line 2435 maps `MsgDefines.MonsterInvasionBossDetail` to `Net.Msgs.MonsterInvasionBossDetailMessge`. `Net/SFSNetwork.luac` `GetMsgType` lines 7-23 requires that mapped module and caches the returned table; incoming handling lines 36-54 creates an instance from the same table and calls `HandleMessage`. The exact detail module has one root local `_uuid`; `OnCreate` lines 12-17 overwrites that upvalue for each send, and successful `HandleMessage` lines 20-30 reuses the same upvalue before forwarding to `MonsterProtectionManager.OnGetDetail`. Therefore overlapping detail requests are not correlation-safe. This explains the owner's observation that one manual Jump/click primes a timer reliably while the previous bulk scanner can miss first-scan timers.

**IMPLEMENTED/OFFLINE-TESTED correction.** Protection detail is now a run-bound serial background queue. The helper requires the exact recovered message module, wraps its `HandleMessage`, and advances only after the current success or error response has actually completed; no bulk send or unresolved-target bulk retry remains. A boss is skipped only when its recovered `createTime + monster_invasion.k12 * 1000` deadline is source-proven already expired against the same `UITimeManager:GetInstance():GetServerTime()` clock used by the game UI; unavailable deadline inputs fail open to requesting detail. AOI acquisition never waits for this optional queue. The prior host-side 100 ms per-AOI protection settle delay is removed. Error/unanswered detail remains unknown and cannot fabricate Remaining.

**Validation and gate.** Lua parses, `git diff --check` is clean, a separate temporary Release build succeeds with zero warnings/errors, and the full deterministic suite reports all six groups true. No live game action was performed for this checkpoint because the owner was actively playing. The next owner check is exact: one Normal Monster scan should return to normal full-world pace, and a shielded Zombie Boss must show Remaining on the **first scan** without Jump/click priming or a second scan. Evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-18-r7-zombie-boss-serial-detail.json`.

### LWB-R7-025 - current-v18 footprint correction and first-scan Zombie Boss Remaining acceptance (2026-09-18)

**LIVE-PROVEN footprint change.** The first fresh v18 acceptance attempt failed before protection enrichment because ordinary native coverage now returned 30 AOIs as 3 consecutive columns x 10 rows, while the R7-012 v17 planner still required at least 40 cells inside a 5x10 footprint. The run was stopped, the exact helper-owned game was closed normally, the journal backup was restored byte-for-byte and current compatibility returned `ok=true`. Production now uses one logical block-column per partial batch and a conservative 3-column full-world stride (340 requests), while retaining the exact 10,000/10,000 AOI union as the final coverage gate.

**LIVE-PROVEN first-scan Remaining source.** The corrected fresh Normal run then completed all 2,500 logical blocks with zero failed/unread blocks in 108.1437786 s and published/reopened exactly 4,735 Monster rows. It found 21 exact Monster-Invasion Zombie Bosses. Source-proven expired bosses required no network detail; 2 bosses required serialized detail, both completed authoritatively, no retry/error occurred, and both persisted future shield deadlines. No Jump, manual boss click or priming second scan occurred. This is the exact automatic behavior required by the owner; the existing frontend `shieldEndTime` countdown consumer therefore has a positive first-scan source.
