Release build and all six deterministic groups pass; frontend generation integrity also passes. Monster is no longer the category gate. Continue Railway/Train on the same shared Manual Scan engine. Evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-18-r7-monster-first-scan-remaining.json`.

### LWB-R7-037 - server jump and first Auto Scan backend-cycle acceptance (2026-09-19)

**RECOVERED current-v19 travel primitives.** Decrypted/decompiled current-v19 `Util/GoToUtil.luac`, `Util/CrossServerUtil.luac`, and `Util/SceneUtils.luac` prove that generic cross-server viewing must not use season-only `GoToUtil.GotoServerZone`. Ordinary travel uses `GoToUtil.GoToServerPreCheck(serverId)` plus `CrossServerUtil.OnCrossServer(serverId)`; return to the login/self server uses `CrossServerUtil.OnBackSelfServer()`. The authoritative live server is `LuaEntry.Player:GetCurServerId()`. Original 0.3.1 public strings independently recover `INVALID_SERVER_ID / server ID must be an integer from 1 to 99999`, `GAME_OPERATION_IN_PROGRESS / another game operation is already in progress`, and `SERVER_JUMP_TIMEOUT / the game did not switch to the target server`.

**Production implementation.** `server_jump` is routed through `ManualMapScanCommandService` and the same owned Overview session/correlation used by map navigation. It is mutually exclusive with active scans and coordinate jumps. The game-side bridge validates profile/session/challenge/PID, performs a truthful same-server no-op, executes the recovered precheck and ordinary cross-server/return path, and reports success only after `GetCurServerId()` equals the requested server. The game-side 15 s timeout and 18 s host envelope are explicitly IMPLEMENTATION POLICY; the original numeric timeout duration is not claimed recovered.

**Live travel proof.** A bounded current-v19 owned-session run first proved same-server no-op, then completed **original -> different target -> original**. The target context was reacquired before the return jump, and the final original context was reacquired after return. The harness exited 0 in 25.11 s and final restoration returned the exact official v19 package.

**First Auto Scan backend cycle.** A second owned current-v19 run executed the same backend sequence used by the recovered Auto Scan frontend: `server_jump(current server)` followed by `map_scan_start` with `scanMode=fast` and only `zombie_boss` selected. It completed 2,500/2,500 logical blocks with zero failed/unread in 19.3625476 s and published 56 Zombie Boss rows. This proved travel no longer blocked the shared Manual engine and that Auto Scan could reuse it without a second scanner. At this historical checkpoint the page scheduler/return behavior was still open; `LWB-R7-038` subsequently live-proves the real parent scheduler and repeated configured return-to-original/status truth.

**Validation / next.** Release build is zero-warning/zero-error, all six deterministic groups return `ok=true`, Lua parse/check-only pass, post-run compatibility is `ok=true`, and no game/launcher/LWBridge process remains. Evidence: [`2026-09-19-r7-server-jump-auto-zombie-cycle.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-19-r7-server-jump-auto-zombie-cycle.json). `LWB-R7-038` subsequently proves the actual parent scheduler plus repeated cross-server return/status. Remaining Auto acceptance is multi-target-list sequencing, disable/stop/failure behavior, and naturally elapsed restart scheduling while continuing to call this same Manual engine.

### LWB-R7-034 - Resource authoritative remaining/full filtering and bootstrap budget (2026-09-18)

**LIVE-PROVEN.** Resource rows now preserve original game Resource naming/config, exact level, configured capacity, gather occupancy and black-tile classification, and additionally acquire authoritative current remaining/full amount through the original `WorldGetDetail` -> `WorldPointDetailManager` path. Production sends a single request per newly observed occupancy-known idle point while LOD0 AOI discovery continues, deduplicates targets, and performs one bounded final cache harvest. Unknown/missing detail remains unknown; `resourceFull=true` is emitted only when authoritative remaining equals full amount.

The final current-v19 Normal acceptance completed 2,500/2,500 logical blocks, zero failed/unread, in 131.208 s at concurrency 8 and published/reopened 838 rows. All 838 had Resource type, level, name key, max amount, known occupancy and known black-tile classification; 56 were occupied. All 782 final idle rows had authoritative detail, yielding 696 full and 86 partial, with zero invalid amount pairs. The downstream default query (`resourceIdleOnly=true`, `resourceFullOnly=true`, `excludeBlackTile=true`) returned 696. No live black-tile Resource existed in this population, so the negative classification path is live-covered while a positive black-tile example remains observational.

**R7-074 live query acceptance, 2026-09-20.** A later Fast full-world run published/reopened 1,788 Resource rows and independently set-compared eight result filters before and after reopen: sampled `resourceNameKey`, `resourceIdleOnly`, `resourceFullOnly`, `excludeBlackTile`, `minLevel`, `maxLevel`, exact level, and the combined default. The live counts were 1,783 idle, 1,665 full, 1,788 known non-black, 420 exact level-3, and 609 rows for the sampled Resource name key. All eight public/current query results exactly matched the independently computed authoritative row sets. The population again had zero black-tile rows, so positive exclusion remains observationally unavailable.

**Performance / route selection.** A live LOD2 whole-world test completed/restored cleanly but returned zero Resource records, so Resource cannot safely reuse Monster/Zombie Boss's one-shot LOD2 discovery. A separate bounded burst sent four uncached native `WorldGetDetail` requests together and received 4/4 in ~1.63 s with no retry, proving Resource amount enrichment can overlap rather than serialize. Full Resource discovery therefore remains on exact LOD0 coverage pending recovery of a different complete native Resource index/path.

**Bootstrap regression found during implementation.** Adding the production detail queue raised `current_live_resource_probe.lua` to 201 simultaneous top-level locals. Lua 5.3's chunk compiler rejected it even though the generic parser accepted the syntax, surfacing externally as missing bridge readiness and `DataCenter.Global.LuaEntry` unavailable. The subsystem is now collapsed behind one top-level runtime table, reducing the count to 192; a deterministic source guard requires `<200`. Fresh v19 bridge readiness and the full Resource acceptance then succeeded. Release build is 0-warning/0-error, all six deterministic groups pass, Lua parse/JS syntax/`git diff --check` are clean, exact official v19 is restored, and no game/recovery journal remains. Evidence: [`2026-09-18-r7-resource-full-truth.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-18-r7-resource-full-truth.json).

## Remaining unknowns

- Exact game-side block scheduling/tick implementation behind `XluaBridgeMapScanTick` after the recovered `startMapScan` request boundary.
- Exact block geometry/order and any mode-specific timing/pacing beyond the recovered `normal=8` / `fast=20` concurrency.
- Exact per-kind point/march field grouping, value types, optionality and map-index normalization.
- Exact current numeric `resourceTypeId` to original public `resourceNameKey` mapping; raw `resourceName` and public `resourceNameKey` are separately recovered but their current-client link is not.
- Current-client/native-capture mapping into the recovered R6-049 `pointIndex`/`mainIndex`/`pointId`/`index`, `marchUuid` and `uuid` builder inputs.
- Retry/unread/failure transition rules.
- Resume cursor/persistence format.
- Native queue capacities and how `dropped` forces scan failure or retry.
- Exact map-index transaction/commit contract and final completion gate.

## Reliability design to implement

Persist progress before acknowledgement. Treat inflight blocks as uncertain after a disconnect and replay them idempotently. Freeze scheduling on bridge loss, save state, perform a clean injection bootstrap, then resume only unresolved work. Completion must require zero unresolved failed/unread blocks, no pending acknowledgements, no dropped native capture, and a stable final commit.

## PM review 12 — R7-003 acceptance boundaries

At `749b8e3`, the two correlated recorded current-client reads and normal-store search/summary are accepted as a bounded acquisition slice. Original-pipe ack/request parity is not required for that slice. Normal UI display and reliable production lifecycle remain open: [PM12-A–D](implementation-handoff.md) address reproduced partial-install rollback failure, shared ownership, cancellation/Stop/timeout, immutable result ingestion and truthful rendered scope/unknown occupancy. The proof runner does not instantiate a window, and both saved successful runs required game stop during restoration. Do not label this full-world scanning, continuous bridge readiness or completed PM10-01. [Review evidence](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-10-pm-review-12.json) separates historical live proof from fresh offline validation.


### LWB-R7-030 - Monster/Zombie Boss fast-only scan and native AOI restore recovery (2026-09-18)

**Failure-first live diagnosis.** The owner reported repeated Monster/Zombie Boss scans that could suddenly become very slow and then stop scanning. The persisted failed run did not lose owned-session identity: it returned `zoom_restore_confirmation_timeout` after the camera tile and `svLod=0` had restored, while `WorldPointManager` AOI geometry remained stuck at the coarse `blockSize=1000/blockCount=1` instead of normal LOD0 `10/100`. Separately, the C# Monster-only route caught coarse timeout/invalid-data/I/O and silently entered the conservative LOD0 world sweep, explaining the dramatic slowdown.

**Correction.** Monster and dedicated Zombie Boss are now fast-only: coarse LOD2 failure is surfaced instead of starting the slow coverage fallback. During zoom restoration the Lua probe waits for the exact original camera tile, LOD0 and 10x100 grid; after three seconds it makes one bounded native `WorldPointManager.UpdateLWAoi_Normal(true)` recovery attempt (reflection, with the normal public update as the bounded fallback call), then rechecks the exact restoration contract. If the second confirmation window still fails, the request terminates; there is no unlimited retry.

**Live acceptance.** A clean Zombie Boss run completed 2,500/2,500 with zero failed/unread blocks in 36.673 s and restored 10x100/LOD0. A dedicated same-owned-session proof then ran Scan twice without restarting Last War: 38.414 s / 30.715 s, 117 / 112 dynamic Zombie Boss rows, both exact 10x100/LOD0 restorations. A controlled confirmation-delay run proved the bounded recovery branch itself. After all test-only forcing was removed, a final clean production run naturally exercised the same recovery path: 2,500/2,500 in 41.053 s, 135 dynamic Zombie Boss rows, `zoomRecoveryAttempted=true`, `zoomRecoveryIssued=true`, method `WorldPointManager.UpdateLWAoi_Normal(true)-reflection`, recovery geometry 10x100/LOD0, stable camera and exact final restoration. Release build is 0-warning/0-error, all six deterministic groups pass, `git diff --check` is clean, current-v18 compatibility is `ok=true`, and cleanup leaves no game/launcher/LWBridge process or recovery journal. Evidence: [`2026-09-18-r7-monster-fast-only-recovery.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-18-r7-monster-fast-only-recovery.json).

### LWB-R7-027 - same-owned-session transient readiness resume (2026-09-18)

**Failure-first live proof.** A controlled 7-second pause of the helper-owned current-v18 game during ordinary Normal Monster acquisition reproduced the remaining resume defect without changing the owned game identity. The scan reached 2,406/2,500 and then consumed 94 logical blocks as `game connection unavailable` failures. Aggregate inspection showed all 94 failed blocks had the same readiness error; the live protocol later continued under the same owned-session identity. The bug was therefore an early full-world resume-anchor gap, not a valid stale-session transition.

**Correction.** Full-world acquisition now establishes its exact run/session `FastFullWorldResumeState` before attempting Monster LOD2. Instantaneous ready-state loss can wait on that immutable owned identity, and LOD2 fallback keeps the anchor for the conservative LOD0 path. Session/challenge/PID/path/start identity mismatch is still rejected fail-closed; no cross-session continuation is permitted. A focused deterministic regression reproduces the early no-ready retry before coarse completion.

**Corrected live acceptance.** Repeating the same 7-second pause completed 2,500/2,500 with zero failed/unread blocks in 18.294 s. The run published and reopened 6,912 Monster rows with Distance on every row, found 56 eligible Zombie Bosses, required 12 serialized detail requests, received 12/12 replies with no retry/error, and persisted 12 active future deadlines. Release build is 0-warning/0-error, all six deterministic groups are green, `git diff --check` is clean, current-v18 compatibility is `ok=true`, exact official v18 is restored, and no game/launcher/LWBridge process or recovery journal remains. Evidence: [`2026-09-18-r7-monster-transient-readiness-resume.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-18-r7-monster-transient-readiness-resume.json).

### LWB-R7-026 - whole-world LOD2 Monster acquisition beats the 60-second target (2026-09-18)

**RECOVERED/LIVE-PROVEN current-v18 geometry.** Current-v18 `WorldPointManager` exposes a coarse LOD transition through the normal public `UpdateViewRequest(true)` path. At the game's maximum zoom, one native request transitions from LOD0 (`blockSize=10`, `blockCount=100`) to LOD2 (`blockSize=1000`, `blockCount=1`). The coarse response contains full `WorldMarch` Monster identity/position/template/level/create-time data rather than only map decorations. A bounded capability proof serialized 7,429 Monster marches, including 67 exact Zombie Boss rows, in 0.347 s. Acceptance requires response-flag transition, LOD2 whole-world geometry, exact return to LOD0 geometry, and exact original camera tile before the snapshot is admitted.

**Production performance and Remaining.** Monster-only ordinary Manual Start now routes its discovery through that one whole-world coarse snapshot, normalizes rows into the existing 2,500 logical block/persistence contract, then runs the already-recovered serialized Monster-Invasion protection queue. Two consecutive live Normal runs completed 2,500/2,500 with zero failures and exact publish/reopen equality in 11.418 s and 10.903 s. They published 7,271 and 7,194 dynamic Monster rows, with Distance on every row. The first run found 63 eligible Zombie Bosses and required 10 detail requests; 10/10 replied and all 10 persisted active future deadlines. The second found 60 eligible bosses, required 9 requests, received 9/9, and persisted 9 active future deadlines. No Jump, click, or priming scan was used.

**Historical safety/fallback at R7-026.** The fast path is accepted only when its session correlation, whole-world LOD2 geometry, exact camera/LOD restoration, Monster normalization, and protection counters all validate. At this checkpoint timeout/validation/I/O failure still fell back to the conservative exact-coverage LOD0 scanner. `LWB-R7-030` supersedes that behavior: Monster/Zombie Boss are fast-only and zoom restoration gets one bounded native AOI-rebuild attempt before a truthful failure. Release build is 0-warning/0-error, all six deterministic groups are green, `git diff --check` is clean, exact official v18 is restored, and no recovery journal/process remains. The owner's 60-second Monster target is therefore technically exceeded. Same-owned-session transient readiness resume was not claimed live at this checkpoint; `LWB-R7-027` subsequently live-proves it. Evidence: [`2026-09-18-r7-monster-lod2-fast-scan.json`](../evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-18-r7-monster-lod2-fast-scan.json).

### LWB-R7-021 - source-backed Zombie Boss deadline survives AOI unload (2026-09-17)

**Owner-positive diagnosis.** The ordinary owner scan now completes 2,500/2,500 blocks with zero failures in 74.791 s and publishes 10,297 Monster rows. The owner screenshot simultaneously shows active Zombie Boss protection timers while Remaining is still `-`. Aggregate inspection found 65 eligible bosses, 65 initial requests, 65 premature retries and 43 authoritative replies. One visibly protected example was already `monsterProtectionKnown=true` / `monsterProtectionActive=true` but had `protectionEndTime=0`; another visible protected example remained unanswered. No live UUID or coordinate is retained.

**Recovered cause and correction.** Current-v18 `MonsterProtection.Refresh` computes `protectionEndTime = march.createTime + DataConfig("monster_invasion","k12") * 1000`; `GetMonsterProtectionEndTime(uuid)` later returns only the manager object's `showEndTime`. During fast scanning the asynchronous reply can arrive after that boss march/troop has unloaded, so the server reply is known/active but the manager getter returns zero. Production now captures the same source deadline while the boss is loaded and uses it only when an authoritative reply says `isProtected=true` and the manager getter is already zero. Inactive or unanswered detail still produces no timer. The 40 ms per-batch retry is removed because the owner run proved it duplicated all 65 requests before first replies arrived; one final retry now targets only still-unanswered UUIDs, followed by the existing bounded final collection.

**Validation / owner gate.** Lua parses, all six deterministic groups pass, Release build is zero-warning/zero-error, packaged Lua matches source, and `git diff --check` is clean. The failed owner lifecycle was restored to exact original v18 and compatibility is `ok=true`. Positive Remaining is not yet claimed fixed until the owner reruns a normal Monster scan and observes a protected Zombie Boss countdown. Evidence: `evidence/lwbridge-implementation/2026-09-17-r7-zombie-boss-deadline-extraction.json`.
